\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\partial_{\beta}Q
and
\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\partial_{\beta}U
respectively, summing over all
|\beta|=K
, and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}+\Lambda-1+K\big)E_{K,R}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\big(\partial_{\beta}Q\,(y\cdot\nabla)\partial_{\beta}Q+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,(y\cdot\nabla)\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\big(\partial_{\beta}Q\,\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla Q)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla U_{i})\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

(3.14)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\alpha\big(\partial_{\beta}Q\,\partial_{\beta}(Q\mathrm{div}\,U)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,\partial_{\beta}(Q\partial_{y_{i}}Q)\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{|\beta|=K}C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\partial_{\beta}\Big(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}L(U)+\frac{\delta}{\alpha}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\Big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle:=-J_{1}-J_{2}-J_{3}+C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}J_{4}.

Integrating by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle-J_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int y\cdot\big(\nabla|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\nabla|\partial_{\beta}U_{i}|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\frac{\mathrm{div}(y\phi^{K}f_{R})}{\phi^{K}f_{R}}\big(|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\Big(3+\frac{Ky\cdot\nabla\phi}{\phi}+\frac{|y|}{Rf_{R}}f^{\prime}(\frac{|y|}{R})\Big)\big(|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

(3.15)

\displaystyle\leq CE_{K,R}+C(K)\int\big(|\nabla^{K}Q|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}U|^{2}\big)|y|\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq CE_{K,R}+C(K)\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{2}.

We now turn to the estimates for
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
.
First, combining the results obtained in Steps 1–2,
we conclude that there exists a constant
C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})
such that
(Q,U)
satisfies

\displaystyle\inf_{(\tau,y)\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}Q(\tau,y)\geq C^{-1}(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*}),

(3.16)

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]}\|(Q-Q^{*},U)\|_{H^{K}}\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*}).

(3.17)

It follows from (
3.17
),
K\geq 4
, and the Sobolev embedding that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{2}\|(\nabla^{j}Q,\nabla^{j}U)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\qquad\text{for all $\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]$}.

(3.18)

Second, in the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.12
), denoting

\varphi=f_{R}^{\frac{1}{4K}}\phi^{\frac{1}{2}},\ \ \psi=1,\ \ p=\infty,\ \ q=2,\ \ l=K,\ \ \bar{r}=\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell},\ \ \theta=\frac{\ell-3/\bar{r}}{K-3/2},

we obtain

\displaystyle\|\langle y\rangle^{-\varepsilon}f_{R}^{\frac{\theta}{4}}\phi^{\frac{K\theta}{2}}\nabla^{\ell}U\|_{L^{\bar{r}}}\leq C(\varepsilon,K)\big(\|U\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1-\theta}\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}U\|_{L^{2}}^{\theta}+\|U\|_{L^{\infty}}\big).

(3.19)

Then, for
\ell+\ell^{*}=K+1
with
\ell,\ \ell^{*}\geq 1
, and
r^{*}=\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell^{*}}
, according to (
3.11
), (
3.19
),
and the Hölder inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}|\nabla^{\ell}U||\nabla^{\ell^{*}}U|\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\big\||\nabla^{\ell}U||\nabla^{\ell^{*}}U|(f_{R}^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}})^{\frac{\ell-3/{\bar{r}}}{K-3/2}}(f_{R}^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}})^{\frac{\ell^{*}-3/{r^{*}}}{K-3/2}}\phi^{-\frac{K}{2(K-3/2)}}f_{R}^{\frac{K-5/2}{4(K-3/2)}}\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big\|\langle y\rangle^{-\frac{1}{10}}(f_{R}^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}})^{\frac{\ell-3/{\bar{r}}}{K-3/2}}\nabla^{\ell}U\big\|_{L^{\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell}}}\big\|\langle y\rangle^{-\frac{1}{10}}(f_{R}^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}})^{\frac{\ell^{*}-3/{r^{*}}}{K-3/2}}\nabla^{\ell^{*}}U\big\|_{L^{\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell^{*}}}}

(3.20)

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})+C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(\int\big(|\nabla^{K}Q|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{K-1/2}{2K-3}}

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\big(1+\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{\frac{K-1/2}{K-3/2}}\big).

Therefore, it follows from the Hölder inequality, (
3.18
), and (
3.2
) that

\displaystyle-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int f_{R}\phi^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla U_{i})\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}U\big\|_{L^{2}}\big(\|U\|_{L^{\infty}}\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\big\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}}\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}U\big\|_{L^{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}U\big\|_{L^{2}}\Big(\sum_{\ell,\ell^{*}\geq 2,\,\ell+\ell^{*}=K+1}\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}|\nabla^{\ell}U||\nabla^{\ell^{*}}U|\big\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

(3.21)

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(1+E_{K,R}+E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\|_{L^{2}}+\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{\frac{K-1/2}{K-3/2}}\big)\Big).

By the same argument as in (
3.2
), we derive

\displaystyle-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\alpha f_{R}\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}Q\,\partial_{\beta}(Q\,\mathrm{div}\,U)\,\mathrm{d}y

(3.22)

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(1+E_{K,R}+E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\|f_{R}^{1/2}\phi^{K/2}\nabla^{K+1}U\|_{L^{2}}+\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{\frac{K-1/2}{K-3/2}}\big)\Big).

Moreover, we obtain from integration by parts, the Hölder inequality, (
3.18
), and (
3.2
) that

\displaystyle-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int f_{R}\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla Q)\partial_{\beta}Q\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int f_{R}\phi^{K}U\cdot\nabla|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y+C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\|\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}}\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}Q\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\|\nabla Q\|_{L^{\infty}}\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}U\big\|_{L^{2}}\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}Q\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}Q\big\|_{L^{2}}\Big(\sum_{\ell,\ell^{*}\geq 2,\ \ell+\ell^{*}=K+1}\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}|\nabla^{\ell}Q||\nabla^{\ell^{*}}U|\big\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

(3.23)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\Big(\mathrm{div}\,U+K\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot U}{\phi}+\frac{U\cdot y}{R|y|f_{R}}f^{\prime}(\frac{|y|}{R})\Big)|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad\,+C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(E_{K,R}+E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{\frac{K-1/2}{K-3/2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(E_{K,R}+E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{\frac{K-1/2}{K-3/2}}\Big),

and

\displaystyle-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int f_{R}\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\partial_{\beta}(Q\nabla Q)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\big|\nabla(f_{R}\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}U)\big|\,\big|\nabla^{K-1}(Q\nabla Q)\big|\,\mathrm{d}y

(3.24)

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\big(E_{K,R}+(1+\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}})\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\big\|_{L^{2}}\big).

Consequently, combining (
3.2
)–(
3.2
) yields

\displaystyle-J_{2}-J_{3}

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(1+E_{K,R}+E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\big\|_{L^{2}}+\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{\frac{K-1/2}{K-3/2}}\big)\Big)

(3.25)

\displaystyle+\,C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\big(1+\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}\big)\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\|_{L^{2}}.

To estimate
J_{4}
, we decompose it into five terms:

\displaystyle J_{4}=

\displaystyle\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\partial_{\beta}\Big(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}L(U)+\frac{\delta}{\alpha}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\Big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\partial_{\beta}L(U)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y\,+\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\big([\partial_{\beta},Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}]L(U)\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\Big([\partial_{\beta},Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}]\big(\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\Big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\big(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}(\partial_{\beta}\nabla Q)\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

(3.26)

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\partial_{\beta}U\cdot Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\,\big(\partial_{\beta}(\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U))-(\partial_{\beta}\nabla Q)\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\,J_{41}+J_{42}+J_{43}+J_{44}+J_{45}.

For
J_{41}
, it follows from integration by parts, (
3.11
), (
3.16
), (
3.18
), and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle J_{41}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\partial_{\beta}L(U)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big(a_{1}|\nabla\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\mathrm{div}\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\big(\nabla(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\phi^{K}\,f_{R})\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\big)\cdot\partial_{\beta}\mathbb{D}(U)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big(a_{1}|\nabla\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\mathrm{div}\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

(3.27)

\displaystyle\quad+C\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big((\frac{\nabla Q}{Q}+\frac{K\nabla\phi}{\phi}+\frac{y}{R|y|f_{R}}f^{\prime}(\frac{|y|}{R}))\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\big)\cdot\partial_{\beta}\mathbb{D}(U)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq-\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big(a_{1}|\nabla^{K+1}U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\mathrm{div}\nabla^{K}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\big\|_{L^{2}}.

To facilitate the estimates of the lower-order derivative terms, we introduce
a family of multi-indices
\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{i}
,
i=0,1,\cdots\!,\ell
for some integer
\ell\in[1,K]
, which satisfy

\begin{split}&|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|+|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}|+\cdots+|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{\ell}|=K+2,\qquad 1\leq|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{i}|\leq K-1\,\,\,\,\,\text{for all $0\leq i\leq\ell$}.\end{split}

(3.28)

Here
|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{i}|
denotes the length of multi-indices
\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{i}
as defined in §
2.1.1
for each
i=0,1,\cdots\!,\ell
.

It follows from the Hölder inequality, (
3.16
), and (
3.19
) that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\big\|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\,\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}}U\,\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q}{Q}\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{2}}Q}{Q}\cdots\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{\ell}}Q}{Q}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|_{L^{2}}

(3.29)

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big\|\langle y\rangle^{-\frac{|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|}{2(K+2)}}\big(f_{R}^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\big)^{\frac{|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|-3/r_{0}}{K-3/2}}\nabla^{|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|}U\Big\|_{L^{\frac{2(K+2)}{|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|}}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{1\leq j\leq\ell}\Big\|\langle y\rangle^{-\frac{|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{j}|}{2(K+2)}}\big(f_{R}^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\big)^{\frac{|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{j}|-3/r_{j}}{K-3/2}}\nabla^{|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{j}|}Q\Big\|_{L^{\frac{2(K+2)}{|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{j}|}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})+C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{\frac{K+1/2}{K-3/2}},

where
r_{j}=\frac{2(K+2)}{|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{j}|},j=0,1,\cdots,\ell
.
Then, based on the Hölder inequality, (
3.16
), (
3.18
), and (
3.28
)–(
3.29
), we have

\displaystyle J_{42}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\big([\partial_{\beta},Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}]L(U)\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})E_{K,R}+C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\,\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{j}|=K+2\\
|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|\geq 3\end{subarray}}\Big\|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}}U\,\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q}{Q}\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{2}}Q}{Q}\cdots\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{\ell}}Q}{Q}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(1+E_{K,R}+E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\big\|_{L^{2}}+\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{\frac{K+1/2}{K-3/2}}\big)\Big).

(3.30)

Following the argument used in (
3.2
), we obtain from the Hölder inequality, (
3.16
), (
3.18
), and (
3.28
)–(
3.29
) that

\displaystyle J_{43}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\big([\partial_{\beta},Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}](\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U))\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(E_{K,R}+E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\,\max_{\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{j}|=K+2}\Big\|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}}U\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q}{Q}\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{2}}Q}{Q}\cdots\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{\ell}}Q}{Q}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(1+E_{K,R}+E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\big\|_{L^{2}}+\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{\frac{K+1/2}{K-3/2}}\big)\Big).

(3.31)

For
J_{44}
, it follows from integration by parts, (
3.16
), (
3.18
), and the Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle J_{44}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\big(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}(\partial_{\beta}\nabla Q)\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=-\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\partial_{\beta}Q\,\mathrm{div}\big(\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\phi^{K}f_{R}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(E_{K,R}+E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\big\|_{L^{2}}\Big).

(3.32)

For
J_{45}
, let
\ell=2
in (
3.28
). Then it follows from (
3.16
), (
3.18
),
and (
3.29
) that

\displaystyle J_{45}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\partial_{\beta}U\cdot Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\,\big(\partial_{\beta}(\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U))-(\partial_{\beta}\nabla Q)\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\phi^{K}f_{R}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(E_{K,R}+E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\big\|_{L^{2}}\Big)

(3.33)

\displaystyle\quad+C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|+|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}|=K+2\\
3\leq|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|\leq K-1\end{subarray}}\Big\|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}}U\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q}{Q}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(1+E_{K,R}+E_{K,R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\big\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\big\|_{L^{2}}+\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{\frac{K+1/2}{K-3/2}}\big)\Big).

Consequently, substituting (
3.2
), (
3.25
), and (
3.2
)–(
3.2
) into (
3.2
), together with (
3.17
) and the Young inequality, yields

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}E_{K,R}+\|f_{R}^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K+1}U\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\Big(1+E_{K,R}+\|(Q,U)\|_{D^{K}}^{\frac{2K+1}{K-3/2}}\Big)

(3.34)

\displaystyle\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})\big(1+E_{K,R}\big).

Applying the Grönwall inequality, we deduce

E_{K,R}(\tau)\leq e^{C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})(\tau-\tau_{0})}E_{K,R}(\tau_{0})+e^{C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})(\tau-\tau_{0})}-1.

By the definition of
E_{K,R}
, we see that
E_{K,R}(\tau_{0})\leq E_{K}(\tau_{0})
. Therefore, for any
R>1
,

E_{K,R}(\tau)\leq e^{C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})(\tau-\tau_{0})}E_{K}(\tau_{0})+e^{C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*})(\tau-\tau_{0})}-1.

(3.35)

Moreover, since

|\nabla^{K}U|^{2}\phi^{K}f_{R}(y)\to|\nabla^{K}U|^{2}\phi^{K}\qquad\mbox{as $R\to\infty$}

for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, Fatou’s lemma yields

\displaystyle\text{ess}\!\!\!\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{*}}E_{K}(\tau)\leq\text{ess}\!\!\!\sup_{\tau_{0}\leq t\leq\tau_{*}}\liminf_{R\to\infty}E_{K,R}(\tau)\leq C(K,Q_{0},U_{0},\tau_{*}).

(3.36)

This completes the proof of Theorem
3.1
.
∎

4.
Choices of the Related Parameters and
Assumptions in Bootstrap Argument

In §
4
–§
6
,
we
establish
the global-in-time energy estimates for the smooth
solutions of the Cauchy problem (
2.12
)–(
2.16
),
restricted to a finite-codimensional manifold of smooth initial data, via a bootstrap argument.
Let
(Q,U)
be the unique smooth solution
of the Cauchy problem (
2.12
)–(
2.16
) on
[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,
whose
local well-posedness has been established in Theorem
3.1
.
In this section, we first fix several parameters determined by
profile
(\overline{Q},\overline{U})
,
which is the smooth spherically symmetric solution to the steady Euler equations
constructed in Lemma
B.2
(see Appendix
B
).
We then introduce the
a priori
assumptions in the bootstrap argument.

4.1.
Choices of the Related Parameters

First, in the rest of this paper, we always assume that

1<\gamma<1+\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}
,
\Lambda\in(1,\Lambda^{*}(\gamma))
is introduced in Lemma
B.1
, and
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
satisfies
\delta_{\rm dis}>0
.
We emphasize that the local well-posedness result established earlier
only imposes a requirement on parameter
K
,
i.e.

K\geq 4
.

Second, we choose some related parameters as follows:

\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\ll\nu_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}\ll\nu_{1}\ll\nu_{0}\ll\frac{1}{E}\ll\frac{1}{K}\ll\frac{1}{m}\ll\eta\ll\nu_{g}=\frac{25}{12}\varepsilon\ll\delta_{\rm{dis}}=O(1),

(4.1)

and parameter
K
is also chosen sufficiently large so that

\quad\frac{1}{K}\ll\tilde{\eta},

(4.2)

where
\varepsilon>0
is the small parameter appearing in Theorem
2.1
,
\widetilde{\eta}
is the constant appearing in Lemma
C.3
, and
\eta
arises from the weight function
defined in (
2.7
). These parameters
\varepsilon,K,E,\nu_{0}
, and
\nu_{1}
are chosen with the hierarchy stated in Remark
2.2
. In particular, we take

K>\max\{3^{m}\,C(J,m),\,500\},

which ensures
K\geq 4
. Therefore, the above assumptions automatically satisfy
the local existence conditions,
and the local solution constructed previously remains valid.

Next, we choose parameter
R_{0}>0
appearing in
(
2.7
) sufficiently large so that

\begin{split}\overline{Q}(y)\geq 2\nu_{0}&\qquad\text{ for all $y\in B(0,R_{0})$,}\\
|\nabla\overline{Q}(y)|+|\nabla\overline{U}(y)|\leq\frac{\nu_{1}}{2},\,\,\,\max\{\overline{Q}(y),|\overline{U}|(y)\}\leq C\nu_{0}&\qquad\text{ for all $y\in B^{c}(0,R_{0}).$}\end{split}

(4.3)

The existence of
R_{0}
follows from
\nu_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}\ll\nu_{1}\ll\nu_{0}\ll 1

and the decay of profile (
B.5
). Therefore,
R_{0}

may be chosen so that

\displaystyle C^{-1}R_{0}^{1-\Lambda}\leq\nu_{0}\leq CR_{0}^{1-\Lambda}.

(4.4)

Moreover, the initial time
\tau_{0}
is chosen sufficiently
large so as to ensure

C(\nu_{0})\leq e^{\delta_{\rm dis}\tau_{0}}.

Finally, parameter
C_{0}
is determined in the following lemma, which plays a key role in the definition of the Hilbert space
X
in §
2.3
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let parameter
C_{0}
be sufficiently large, which depends only on
\Lambda,\alpha
,
the self-similar profile
(\overline{Q},\overline{U})
, and the smooth cutoff function
\widehat{X}
.
Then profile
(\overline{Q},\overline{U})
satisfies the following conditions for all
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
:

\displaystyle|\alpha\widehat{X}\overline{Q}|+|\widehat{X}\overline{U}|+|\alpha\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|+|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{100C_{1}}\quad\,\text{ for $y\in B^{c}(0,C_{0})$},

(4.5)

\displaystyle\|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{20000},

(4.6)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{8}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{8}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{20000},

(4.7)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=3}^{5}(\|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{20000},

(4.8)

where
C_{1}
is determined by

\frac{1}{100}=\frac{32}{\Lambda-1}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\Big(\frac{1}{100}\Big)^{\frac{1}{20}}.

(4.9)

Proof.

Since
|\widehat{X}|\leq 1
and
|\nabla\widehat{X}|\leq 2e^{-\tau}
, (
4.5
) and (
4.6
) follow directly from the decay properties of
(\overline{Q},\overline{U})
stated in (
B.5
).

We next verify (
4.7
) and (
4.8
). For those terms in which no derivative falls on the cutoff function
\widehat{X}
, the desired decay again follows immediately from (
B.5
).
Moreover, from the definition of
\widehat{X}
, we have

\text{supp}(\nabla\widehat{X})\subset B(0,e^{\tau})\cap B^{c}\big(0,\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\big),\qquad|\nabla^{j}\widehat{X}|\leq C(j)e^{-j\tau}.

Then, for
|\nabla^{j}\widehat{X}||\nabla^{k}\overline{Q}|
,

\displaystyle\||\nabla^{j}\widehat{X}||\nabla^{k}\overline{Q}|\|^{q}_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C(j,k,q)e^{-jq\tau}\int_{\frac{1}{2}e^{\tau}}^{e^{\tau}}r^{-q(\Lambda-1)-qk+2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C(j,k,q)e^{-jq\tau}e^{(-q(\Lambda-1)-qk+3)\tau}

\displaystyle\leq C(j,k,q)e^{-(2q-3)\tau}\leq C(j,k,q)e^{-\tau}.

Since
j\geq 1
,
k\geq 0
,
2\leq j+k\leq 5
,
q\in\{2,8\}
,
and
\tau\geq\tau_{0}\gg 1
,
those terms with at least one derivative on
\widehat{X}
are sufficiently small.
∎

Here the
L^{8}
–estimate (
4.7
) will be used in the energy estimate of

\max\big\{\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))},\ \|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big\}

through the Hölder inequality.

4.2.
Assumptions in the Bootstrap Argument

We first introduce the initial perturbation

(\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{U}_{0}):=(Q_{0}-\widehat{X}\overline{Q},\,U_{0}-\widehat{X}\overline{U}),

which will be used throughout the subsequent analysis,
where
\widehat{X}
denotes the cutoff function introduced in (
2.6
).

According to Lemma
C.3
, the Hilbert space
X
admits the direct sum decomposition

X=V_{\rm sta}\oplus V_{\rm uns},

where
V_{\rm sta}
and
V_{\rm uns}
denote the stable and unstable subspaces associated with the truncated linear operator
\mathcal{L}
, respectively.

Since space
X
consists of functions supported in ball
B(0,3C_{0})\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
, whereas perturbation

(\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{U}_{0})
is defined on the whole space

\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we first localize the perturbation by using the cutoff
function
\chi_{2}
that is defined in §
2.1.3
. We then write the initial perturbation

(\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{U}_{0})
as the sum of a stable part
(\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*},\widetilde{U}_{0}^{*})

and an unstable component, where the stable part is chosen such that

(\chi_{2}\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*},\,\chi_{2}\widetilde{U}_{0}^{*})\in V_{\rm sta}.

(4.10)

On the other hand, the unstable subspace
V_{\rm uns}
is finite-dimensional.
Let
\{(\varphi_{i,\zeta},\varphi_{i,u})\}_{i=1}^{N}
be an orthonormal basis of
V_{\rm uns}
constructed in Lemma
C.3
in Appendix
C
. The initial perturbation can be expressed as

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{0}=\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{k}_{i}\varphi_{i,\zeta},\\
\displaystyle\widetilde{U}_{0}=\widetilde{U}_{0}^{*}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{k}_{i}\varphi_{i,u},\end{cases}

(4.11)

where coefficients
\{\hat{k}_{i}\}
parameterize the unstable component of the initial data and will be chosen later so as to control the growth of the unstable modes.

Moreover, the stable component

(\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*},\widetilde{U}_{0}^{*})

is chosen such that the full initial perturbation

(\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{U}_{0})

satisfies the following conditions:

\begin{split}\max\big\{\|\widetilde{Q}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}},\,\|\widetilde{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}},\,\|\chi_{2}\widetilde{Q}^{*}_{0}\|_{X},\,\|\chi_{2}\widetilde{U}^{*}_{0}\|_{X}\big\}\leq\nu_{1},\\
E_{K}(\tau_{0})\leq\frac{E}{2},\quad\,\widetilde{Q}_{0}+\widehat{X}\overline{Q}\geq\frac{\nu_{1}}{2}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{1-\Lambda}.\end{split}

(4.12)

In addition, the initial data are assumed to satisfy the spatial decay condition:

\langle y\rangle^{\Lambda}\big(|\nabla(\widetilde{Q}_{0}+\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|+|\nabla(\widetilde{U}_{0}+\widehat{X}\overline{U})|\big)<\infty.

(4.13)

In order to use the analysis for the truncated linear operator

\mathcal{L}=(\mathcal{L}_{\zeta},\mathcal{L}_{u})=\chi_{2}(\mathcal{L}_{\zeta}^{e},\mathcal{L}_{u}^{e})-J(1-\chi_{1}),

we consider an additional system, which we call the
truncated equations
,
i.e.
,
\eqref{eq:truncated}_{1}
–
\eqref{eq:truncated}_{2}
. Now we consider the corresponding Cauchy problem:

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\overset{\approx}{Q}=\mathcal{L}_{\zeta}(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})+\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}_{\zeta}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U}),\\
\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\overset{\approx}{U}=\mathcal{L}_{u}(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})+\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}_{u}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U}),\\
\displaystyle(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})(\tau_{0},y)=(\overset{\approx}{Q}_{0},\overset{\approx}{U}_{0})(y)\qquad\text{for $y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}$},\\
(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})(\tau,y)\to(0,0)\qquad\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \text{as $|y|\to\infty$ \, for $\tau\geq\tau_{0}$},\end{cases}

(4.14)

with
\mathcal{F}_{\zeta}=\mathcal{N}_{\zeta}+\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}
and
\mathcal{F}_{u}=\mathcal{N}_{u}+\mathcal{E}_{u}+\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}
,
where
(\overset{\approx}{Q}_{0},\overset{\approx}{U}_{0})
are given as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\overset{\approx}{Q}_{0}=\chi_{2}\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{k}_{i}\varphi_{i,\zeta},\\
\displaystyle\overset{\approx}{U}_{0}=\chi_{2}\widetilde{U}_{0}^{*}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{k}_{i}\varphi_{i,u}.\end{cases}

(4.15)

Remark 4.1
.

Equations (
4.14
)
1
–
(
4.14
)
2
constitute a first-order symmetric hyperbolic system. Since
Theorem
3.1
ensures that
(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U})=(Q-\widehat{X}\overline{Q},\,U-\widehat{X}\overline{U})
is a smooth vector function on
interval
[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
, the standard well-posedness theory for symmetric hyperbolic systems
(
see
§7.3
in
[
16
]
)
implies that the Cauchy problem (
4.14
)
–
(
4.15
) admits a unique smooth solution
(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})
in
[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

An important relation between
(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})
and
(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U})
is observed in the following lemma:

Lemma 4.2
.

Suppose that
(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})
is a smooth solution to (
4.14
)
in
[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
obtained in
Remark
4.1
.
If, at
\tau=\tau_{0}
,

(\overset{\approx}{Q}_{0},\overset{\approx}{U}_{0})=(\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{U}_{0})\qquad\text{for all $y\in B(0,C_{0})$},

then

(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})=(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U})\qquad\text{ for all $(\tau,y)\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]\times B(0,C_{0})$}.

Proof.

Denote

(\delta_{Q},\delta_{U})=(\widetilde{Q}-\overset{\approx}{Q},\,\widetilde{U}-\overset{\approx}{U}).

Then we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\delta_{Q}+(\Lambda-1)\delta_{Q}

\displaystyle=-(y+\widehat{X}\overline{U})\cdot\nabla\delta_{Q}-\alpha(\widehat{X}\overline{S})\,\mathrm{div}\,\delta_{U}-\delta_{U}\cdot\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})-\alpha\delta_{Q}\,\mathrm{div}(\widehat{X}\overline{U}),

(4.16)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\delta_{U}+(\Lambda-1)\delta_{U}

\displaystyle=-(y+\widehat{X}\overline{U})\cdot\nabla\delta_{U}-\alpha(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\nabla\delta_{Q}-\delta_{U}\cdot\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U})-\alpha\delta_{Q}\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})

in a ball
B(0,C_{0})
and
(\delta_{Q},\delta_{U})|_{\tau=\tau_{0}}=(0,0)
.

Notice that profile
(\overline{Q}(y),\overline{U}(y))
is spherically symmetric.
Then there is a scalar function
\overline{\mathcal{U}}(|y|)
such that

(\overline{Q},\overline{U})(y)=(\overline{Q}(|y|),\,\overline{\mathcal{U}}(|y|)\frac{y}{|y|}).

(4.17)

Multiplying (
4.16
)
1
and (
4.16
)
2
by
\delta_{Q}
and
\delta_{U}
respectively, summing them up and integrating over
B(0,C_{0})
,
it follows from (
4.17
)
and the main properties of
(\overline{Q}(y),\,\overline{U}(y),\,\overline{\mathcal{U}}(|y|))

shown in Lemma
B.3
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}\tau}\int_{B(0,C_{0})}\big(\delta_{Q}^{2}+|\delta_{U}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{B(0,C_{0})}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(y+\overline{U})\cdot\nabla(\delta_{Q}^{2})-\alpha\overline{Q}\delta_{Q}\,\mathrm{div}\,\delta_{U}-(\Lambda-1)\delta_{Q}^{2}-\delta_{Q}\delta_{U}\cdot\nabla\overline{Q}-\alpha\delta_{Q}^{2}\,\mathrm{div}\,\overline{U}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{B(0,C_{0})}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}(y+\overline{U})\cdot\nabla(|\delta_{U}|^{2})-\alpha\overline{Q}\nabla\delta_{Q}\cdot\delta_{U}-(\Lambda-1)|\delta_{U}|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-(\delta_{U}\cdot\nabla)\overline{U}\cdot\delta_{U}-\alpha\delta_{Q}\nabla\overline{Q}\cdot\delta_{U}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{B(0,C_{0})}\Big(\frac{3+\mathrm{div}\,\overline{U}}{2}\big(\delta_{Q}^{2}+|\delta_{U}|^{2}\big)+\alpha\nabla\overline{Q}\cdot(\delta_{Q}\delta_{U})-(\Lambda-1)\big(\delta_{Q}^{2}+|\delta_{U}|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\quad-\delta_{U}\cdot\nabla\overline{U}\cdot\delta_{U}-(\alpha+1)\delta_{Q}\delta_{U}\cdot\nabla\overline{Q}-\alpha\delta_{Q}^{2}\,\mathrm{div}\,\overline{U}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad\,\,-\int_{\partial B(0,C_{0})}\Big(\frac{|y|+\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{2}\big(\delta_{Q}^{2}+|\delta_{U}|^{2}\big)+\alpha\overline{Q}\delta_{Q}(\delta_{U}\cdot\bm{n})\Big)\,\text{d}S

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{B(0,C_{0})}\big(\delta_{Q}^{2}+|\delta_{U}|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y-\int_{\partial B(0,C_{0})}\frac{|y|+\overline{\mathcal{U}}-\alpha\overline{Q}}{2}\big(\delta_{Q}^{2}+|\delta_{U}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}S

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{B(0,C_{0})}\big(\delta_{Q}^{2}+|\delta_{U}|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y,

where
\bm{n}
is the outward unit normal vector to
\partial B(0,C_{0})
.
It follows from the Grönwall inequality and
(\delta_{Q},\,\delta_{U})|_{\tau=\tau_{0}}=(0,0)
in
B(0,C_{0})
that

(\delta_{Q},\,\delta_{U})(\tau,y)=(0,0)\qquad\text{ for all $(\tau,y)\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]\times B(0,C_{0})$}.

∎

To establish all the required estimates, it suffices to prove Proposition
4.1
.

Proposition 4.1
.

For the initial data satisfying (
4.10
)
–
(
4.13
),
assume that (
4.1
)
–
(
4.4
) hold and that, for all
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
, the following estimates hold
:

\displaystyle\|P_{\rm{uns}}(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})\|_{X}

\displaystyle\leq\nu_{1}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})},

(4.18)

\displaystyle\max\big\{|\widetilde{Q}|,\ |\widetilde{U}|\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{0}}{100}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})},

(4.19)

\displaystyle\max\big\{\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))},\ \|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\nu_{0}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})},

(4.20)

\displaystyle E_{K}=\int\big(|\nabla^{K}Q|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq E.

(4.21)

Then, for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
,

\displaystyle\|P_{\rm{uns}}(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})\|_{X}

\displaystyle\leq\nu_{1}^{\frac{21}{20}}e^{-\frac{4\varepsilon}{3}(\tau-\tau_{0})},

(4.22)

\displaystyle\max\big\{|\widetilde{Q}|,\ |\widetilde{U}|\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{0}}{200}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})},

(4.23)

\displaystyle\max\big\{\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))},\ \|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\nu_{0}}{2}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})},

(4.24)

\displaystyle E_{K}=\int\big(|\nabla^{K}Q|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,{\rm d}y

\displaystyle\leq\frac{E}{2}.

(4.25)

Here
P_{\rm{uns}}
is a projection onto
V_{\rm{uns}}
described in
§
2.1.2
.

The proof of Proposition
4.1
is carried out via a bootstrap argument, whose details are presented in §
6
. Specifically, estimate (
4.23
) follows from Lemmas
6.3
–
6.4
, while (
4.24
) is a consequence of Lemmas
6.3
and
6.5
. The higher-order estimate (
4.25
) is established in Lemma
6.7
. Finally, the control of the unstable modes (
4.22
) is proved in §
6.1.3
.

5.
Spatial Decay Estimates

In this section, we derive the spatial decay estimates under the bootstrap assumptions introduced above.
The derived decay rates allow us to complete the bootstrap argument in the next section.
In this section, the initial data function
(\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{U}_{0})
under consideration
is constructed in (
4.10
)–(
4.13
),

1<\gamma<1+\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}
,
\Lambda\in(1,\Lambda^{*}(\gamma))
is introduced in Lemma
B.1
,
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
satisfies
\delta_{\rm dis}>0
,
and assumptions (
4.1
)–(
4.4
) and (
4.18
)–(
4.21
)
always hold.

We first obtain an energy estimate of perturbation
(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U})
.

Lemma 5.1
.

For all
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
,

\widetilde{E}_{K}(\tau):=\int\big(|\nabla^{K}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}\widetilde{U}|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}{\rm d}y\leq 2E.

(5.1)

Proof.

Since
(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U})=(Q,U)-(\widehat{X}\overline{Q},\widehat{X}\overline{U})

and
K\ll E
, from (
4.21
), we only need to show

\int\big(|\nabla^{K}\big(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\text{d}y\leq C(K).

(5.2)

The decay of profile (
B.5
) and the definition of
\phi
(
2.7
) lead to

\big(|\nabla^{K}\overline{Q}|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}\overline{U}|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\leq C(K)\langle y\rangle^{-2(\Lambda-1)-2K\eta}.

Since
\frac{1}{K}\ll\eta
,
we claim

\int\big(|\nabla^{K}\overline{Q}|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}\overline{U}|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\text{d}y\leq C(K).

(5.3)

The definition of
\widehat{X}
gives

\text{supp}\,\widehat{X}\subset B(0,e^{\tau}),\,\,\,\,\text{supp}\,(1-\widehat{X})\subset B^{c}\big(0,\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\big),\,\,\,\,\text{supp}\,\nabla\widehat{X}\subset B(0,e^{\tau})\cap B^{c}\big(0,\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\big),

(5.4)

and

\|\nabla^{j}\widehat{X}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(j)e^{-j\tau}.

(5.5)

Applying (
2.7
), (
5.4
)–(
5.5
), and the decay properties of
(\overline{Q},\overline{U})
stated in (
B.5
) yields

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq j\leq K-1}\int|\nabla^{K-j}\widehat{X}|^{2}\big(|\nabla^{j}\overline{Q}|^{2}+|\nabla^{j}\overline{U}|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\sum_{0\leq j\leq K-1}e^{-2(K-j)\tau}\int_{\frac{1}{2}e^{\tau}}^{e^{\tau}}r^{-2(\Lambda-1)-2j}r^{2(1-\eta)K}r^{2}\text{d}r

(5.6)

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\sum_{0\leq j\leq K-1}e^{-2(K-j)\tau+(3+2(1-\eta)K-2(\Lambda-1)-2j)\tau}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)e^{-(2K\eta-3+2(\Lambda-1))\tau}\leq C(K).

Thus, (
5.2
) follows from (
5.3
) and (
5
).
∎

We now establish pointwise lower and upper bounds for
Q
.

Lemma 5.2
.

When
y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

Q\geq\frac{\nu_{1}}{C}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-(\Lambda-1)}\qquad\text{for }\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}].

When
|y|\geq R_{0}
,

Q\leq C\nu_{0}\max\big\{\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-(\Lambda-1)},\,e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})(\Lambda-1)}\big\}\qquad\text{for }\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}].

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1.
For
|y|<R_{0}
, it follows from (
4.3
) and (
4.19
) that
Q\geq 2\nu_{0}-\|\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}\geq\nu_{0}
.

2.
Define
\omega_{Q,y_{0}}(\tau):=e^{(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}Q(\tau,y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})})
. It follows from (
2.12
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\omega_{Q,y_{0}}(\tau)

\displaystyle=(\Lambda-1)e^{(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}Q(\tau,y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})})+e^{(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\partial_{\tau}Q(\tau,y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})})

(5.7)

\displaystyle+e^{(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\cdot\nabla Q(\tau,y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})})

\displaystyle=-e^{(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}(U\cdot\nabla Q)(\tau,y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})})-\alpha e^{(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}(Q\,\mathrm{div}\,U)(\tau,y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})}).

It follows from (
4.1
), (
4.19
), (
5.1
), and the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.10
) that

|\nabla\widetilde{Q}|\leq C(K)\big(\|\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1-\frac{1}{K-3/2}}\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{K-3/2}}\phi^{\frac{-K}{2K-3}}+\|\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\langle y\rangle^{-1}\big),

which leads to

\|\phi^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(K)\big(\nu_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{K-3/2}}E^{\frac{1}{2K-3}}+\nu_{0}\big)\leq\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}}.

(5.8)

Moreover, for
|y|\geq R_{0}
, we obtain from (
B.5
), (
4.4
), and (
5.4
)–(
5.5
) that

\displaystyle\phi^{\frac{1}{2}}|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{|y|}{R_{0}}\Big)^{1-\eta}\big(|\nabla\widehat{X}\overline{Q}|+|\widehat{X}\nabla\overline{Q}|\big)

(5.9)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{|y|}{R_{0}}\frac{1}{|y|^{\Lambda}}\leq\frac{C}{R_{0}^{\Lambda}}\leq C\nu_{0}.

Combining (
5.8
)–(
5.9
) gives

\|\phi^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla Q\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,R_{0}))}\leq\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}}.

(5.10)

By the same argument, it also follows that

\|\phi^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,R_{0}))}\leq\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}}.

(5.11)

Thus, we obtain from (
1.14
), (
4.1
), (
4.3
), (
4.19
), and (
5.10
)–(
5.11
) that

\displaystyle\big|e^{(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}U(\tau,y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})})\nabla Q(\tau,y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\frac{y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})}}{R_{0}}\big)^{-1+\eta}e^{(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\nu_{0}^{\frac{19}{10}}

\displaystyle\leq C\nu_{0}^{\frac{19}{10}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-1+\eta}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})(\eta+\Lambda-2)}\leq\frac{1}{2}\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{1-\Lambda}e^{-\frac{\tau-\bar{\tau}}{10}},

and

|\alpha e^{(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}Q(\tau,y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})})\,\mathrm{div}U(\tau,y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})})|\leq\frac{1}{2}\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{1-\Lambda}e^{-\frac{\tau-\bar{\tau}}{10}}.

Collecting the above two inequalities yields

|\partial_{\tau}\omega_{Q,y_{0}}(\tau)|\leq\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{1-\Lambda}e^{-\frac{\tau-\bar{\tau}}{10}}.

(5.12)

Integrating (
5.12
) over
[\bar{\tau},\tau]
, we obtain

|\omega_{Q,y_{0}}(\tau)-\omega_{Q,y_{0}}(\bar{\tau})|\leq\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{1-\Lambda},

(5.13)

which holds uniformly for all
\tau\geq\bar{\tau}
.

3.
Since
\overline{Q}
is smooth in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, it follows from (
4.3
) that

2\nu_{0}\leq\overline{Q}\leq C\nu_{0}\qquad\text{ for $|y|=R_{0}$}.

Consequently,

\nu_{0}\leq\omega_{Q,y_{0}}(\bar{\tau})\leq C\nu_{0}\qquad\text{ for $|y_{0}|=R_{0}$}.

By (
5.13
), we obtain that, for all
\tau\geq\bar{\tau}
,

\frac{1}{2}\nu_{0}\leq\omega_{Q,y_{0}}(\tau)\leq C\nu_{0}\qquad\text{ for $|y_{0}|=R_{0}$}.

Equivalently,

\frac{1}{C}\nu_{0}e^{-(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\leq Q(\tau,y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})})\leq C\nu_{0}e^{-(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}.

(5.14)

Noting that
\frac{y}{y_{0}}=e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}
, we have

\frac{\nu_{0}}{C}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-(\Lambda-1)}\leq Q(\tau,y)\leq C\nu_{0}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-(\Lambda-1)}.

(5.15)

Next, taking
\bar{\tau}=\tau_{0}
and considering
|y_{0}|\geq R_{0}
, it follows
from the definition of the initial data (
4.12
) that

\frac{\nu_{1}}{2}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{1-\Lambda}\leq Q(\tau_{0},y_{0})\leq C\nu_{0}.

Therefore, we have

e^{-(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\tau_{0})}\frac{\nu_{1}}{C}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{1-\Lambda}\leq Q(\tau,y)=e^{-(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\tau_{0})}w_{Q,y_{0}}(\tau)\leq C\nu_{0}e^{-(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

(5.16)

The upper bound follows directly. For the lower bound, we use the identity
e^{(1-\Lambda)(\tau-\tau_{0})}=(\frac{y}{y_{0}})^{1-\Lambda}
. This completes the proof.
∎

We next derive pointwise decay estimates for the first-order spatial derivatives of solution
(Q,U)
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For all
(\tau,y)\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\displaystyle|\nabla\widetilde{Q}|+|\nabla Q|\leq C\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\Lambda},

(5.17)

\displaystyle|\nabla\widetilde{U}|+|\nabla U|\leq C\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\Lambda}.

(5.18)

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

1
.
Bounds of
Q
,
\widetilde{Q}
,
U
, and
\widetilde{U}
when
|y|\leq R_{0}
.

For
|y|\leq R_{0}
, it follows from (
4.1
) and the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.10
) between
(
4.19
) and
(
5.1
) with

\varphi=\phi^{\frac{1}{2}},\,\,\,l=K,\,\,\,i=1,\,\,\,\theta=\frac{1}{K-3/2},

that

|\nabla\widetilde{Q}|+|\nabla\widetilde{U}|\leq C(K)\big(\nu_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{K-3/2}}E^{\frac{1}{2K-3}}\phi^{-\frac{K}{2(K-3/2)}}+\nu_{0}\langle y\rangle^{-j}\big)\leq C(K)\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}}\leq C\leq C\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\Lambda}.

This, together with the properties of

(\widehat{X}\overline{Q},\,\widehat{X}\overline{U})
stated in (
B.5
), yields (
5.18
).

2
.
Bounds of
Q
and
\widetilde{Q}
when
|y|\geq R_{0}
.

In the exterior region
B^{c}(0,R_{0})
, (
4.3
), and (
4.19
) yield

\max\{|Q|,|U|\}\leq C\nu_{0}
. We then interpolate these bounds with (
4.21
). More precisely, applying the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.10
) with

\varphi=\phi^{\frac{1}{2}},\,\,\,l=K,\,\,\,i=j,\,\,\,\theta=\frac{j}{K-3/2},

yields that, for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
,

|\nabla^{j}Q|+|\nabla^{j}U|\leq C(K)\big(\nu_{0}^{1-\frac{j}{K-3/2}}E^{\frac{j}{2K-3}}\phi^{-\frac{Kj}{2(K-3/2)}}+\nu_{0}\langle y\rangle^{-j}\big).

We obtain from (
4.1
) that

|\nabla Q|+|\nabla U|\leq\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}}\phi^{-\frac{1}{2}},\quad|\nabla^{2}Q|+|\nabla^{2}U|\leq\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}}\phi^{-1},\quad|\nabla^{3}Q|+|\nabla^{3}U|\leq\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}}\phi^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

(5.19)

Applying
\partial_{y_{i}}
to
\eqref{selfsimilar eq}_{1}
yields

\partial_{\tau}\partial_{y_{i}}Q=-\Lambda\partial_{y_{i}}Q-y\cdot\nabla\partial_{y_{i}}Q-\partial_{y_{i}}(U\cdot\nabla Q)-\alpha\partial_{y_{i}}(Q\,\mathrm{div}\,U).

For any
|y|\geq R_{0}
, we consider
y=y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})}
,
where either
\bar{\tau}=\tau_{0}
or
|y_{0}|=R_{0}
. Fix
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, define

\omega_{Q,y_{0},i}(\tau):=e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\partial_{y_{i}}Q(\tau,y_{0}e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}).

Then
\omega_{Q,y_{0},i}
satisfies

\partial_{\tau}\omega_{Q,y_{0},i}=-e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\big(\partial_{y_{i}}U\cdot\nabla Q+U\cdot\nabla\partial_{y_{i}}Q+\alpha\partial_{y_{i}}Q\,\mathrm{div}\,U+\alpha Q\,\mathrm{div}(\partial_{y_{i}}U)\big),

where all the terms are evaluated at
(\tau,\,y_{0}e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}})
. We obtain from
|Q|,|U|\leq C\nu_{0}
and (
5.19
) that

|\partial_{\tau}\omega_{Q,y_{0},i}|\leq C\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}\phi(y_{0}e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}})^{-1}e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\leq C\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}e^{(\Lambda-2+2\eta)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-2+2\eta}.

Consequently,

\begin{split}&\big|\partial_{y_{i}}Q(\tau,y_{0}e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}})-e^{-\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\partial_{y_{i}}Q(\bar{\tau},y_{0}))\big|\\
&=e^{-\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}|\omega_{Q,y_{0},i}(\tau)-\omega_{Q,y_{0},i}(\bar{\tau})|\leq C\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}e^{-\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\Lambda}.\end{split}

It follows from (
4.4
) and (
4.13
) that, for all
|y_{0}|\geq R_{0}
,

|\partial_{y_{i}}Q(\tau_{0},y_{0})|\leq C\langle y_{0}\rangle^{-\Lambda}\leq C\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\rangle^{-\Lambda}.

Moreover, by the result obtained in Step 1, we have

|\partial_{y_{i}}Q(\bar{\tau},y_{0})|\leq C\qquad\,\text{for $|y_{0}|=R_{0}$ and $\bar{\tau}\geq\tau_{0}$}.

Combining these estimates, we derive that, for all
|y|\geq R_{0}
,

|\nabla Q|\leq C\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\Lambda}.

(5.20)

For
\nabla\widetilde{Q}
, it suffices to prove

|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|\leq C\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\rangle^{-\Lambda}.

Since

\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})=\nabla\overline{Q}\widehat{X}+\nabla\widehat{X}\overline{Q},

the first term inherits the decay of
\overline{Q}
, while the second term is controlled using the definition of
\widehat{X}
, which yields the desired decay.

3
.
Bounds of
U
and
\widetilde{U}
when
|y|\geq R_{0}
.
Applying
\partial_{y_{i}}
to
\eqref{selfsimilar eq}_{2}
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\partial_{y_{i}}U=

\displaystyle-\Lambda\partial_{y_{i}}U-y\cdot\nabla\partial_{y_{i}}U-\partial_{y_{i}}U\cdot\nabla U-U\cdot\nabla(\partial_{y_{i}}U)-\alpha\partial_{y_{i}}Q\nabla Q-\alpha Q\nabla(\partial_{y_{i}}Q)

\displaystyle+C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\Big(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}L(\partial_{y_{i}}U)+\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\partial_{y_{i}}QL(U)+\frac{\delta}{\alpha}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(\partial_{y_{i}}U)\Big.

\displaystyle+\Big.\frac{\delta}{\alpha}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nabla(\partial_{y_{i}}Q)\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)+\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\big(\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1\big)Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-2}\,\partial_{y_{i}}Q\,\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\Big).

For any
|y|\geq R_{0}
, we consider
y=y_{0}e^{(\tau-\bar{\tau})}
for either
\bar{\tau}=\tau_{0}
or
|y_{0}|=R_{0}
. Fix
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, define

\omega_{U,y_{0},i}(\tau):=e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\partial_{y_{i}}U(\tau,y_{0}e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}).

Then
\omega_{U,y_{0},i}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\omega_{U,y_{0},i}=

\displaystyle-e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\big((\partial_{y_{i}}U\cdot\nabla)U+(U\cdot\nabla)(\partial_{y_{i}}U)+\alpha\partial_{y_{i}}Q\nabla Q+\alpha Q\nabla(\partial_{y_{i}}Q)\big)

\displaystyle+C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\Big(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}L(\partial_{y_{i}}U)+\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\partial_{y_{i}}QL(U)

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{\alpha}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(\partial_{y_{i}}U)\Big.+\Big.\frac{\delta}{\alpha}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nabla(\partial_{y_{i}}Q)\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\big(\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1\big)Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-2}\,\partial_{y_{i}}Q\,\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\Big).

It follows from (
2.14
), (
4.3
), (
4.19
), Lemma
5.2
, and (
5.19
)–(
5.20
) that

\displaystyle e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\big|(\partial_{y_{i}}U\cdot\nabla)U+(U\cdot\nabla)(\partial_{y_{i}}U)+\alpha\partial_{y_{i}}Q\nabla Q+\alpha Q\nabla(\partial_{y_{i}}Q)\big|

\displaystyle\,\,\,\leq C\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}e^{(\Lambda-2+2\eta)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-2+2\eta},

(5.21)

\displaystyle e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\big|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}L(\partial_{y_{i}}U)\big|

\displaystyle\,\,\,\leq Ce^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\bar{\tau}}\nu_{1}^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-1+3\eta+\delta_{\rm{dis}}-\Lambda}e^{(-1+3\eta)(\tau-\bar{\tau})},

(5.22)

\displaystyle e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\big|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\partial_{y_{i}}QL(U)+Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(\partial_{y_{i}}U)\big|

\displaystyle\,\,\,\leq Ce^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\nu_{1}^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}+1}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-3+2\eta+(\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha})(\Lambda-1)}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\leq Ce^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\bar{\tau}}\nu_{1}^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nu_{0}^{\frac{19}{10}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-1+2\eta+\delta_{\rm{dis}}-\Lambda}e^{(-1+2\eta)(\tau-\bar{\tau})},

(5.23)

\displaystyle e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\big|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-2}\,\partial_{y_{i}}Q\,\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big|

\displaystyle\,\,\,\leq Ce^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\nu_{1}^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-2}\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}+2}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-3+\eta+(\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha})(\Lambda-1)}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\leq Ce^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\bar{\tau}}\nu_{1}^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-2}\nu_{0}^{\frac{29}{10}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-1+\eta+\delta_{\rm{dis}}-\Lambda}e^{(-1+\eta)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}.

(5.24)

For the remaining term, we apply (
4.1
), (
4.4
), (
4.21
), (
5.20
), and the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.9
) between
\|\langle y\rangle^{\Lambda}\nabla Q\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}Q\|_{L^{2}}
with

\varphi=\phi^{\frac{K}{2(K-1)}},\ \ \psi=\langle y\rangle^{\frac{\Lambda}{K-1}},\ \ i=1,\ \ p=\infty,\ \ q=2,\ \ l=K-1,\ \ \theta=\frac{1}{K-5/2},

to obtain

\displaystyle|\nabla^{2}Q|

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\|\langle y\rangle^{\Lambda}\nabla Q\|^{1-\frac{1}{K-5/2}}_{L^{\infty}}\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}Q\|^{\frac{1}{K-5/2}}_{L^{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-\Lambda(1-\frac{1}{K-5/2})}\phi^{-\frac{K}{2(K-5/2)}}

\displaystyle\quad+C(K)\|\langle y\rangle^{\Lambda}\nabla Q\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\langle y\rangle^{-1-\Lambda}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\Big(E^{\frac{1}{2K-5}}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\Lambda(1-\frac{1}{K-5/2})}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\frac{K(1-\eta)}{K-5/2}}+\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-1-\Lambda}\Big)

(5.25)

\displaystyle=C(K)\Big(E^{\frac{1}{2K-5}}\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\rangle^{-1+\eta-\Lambda}\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\rangle^{\frac{2\Lambda-5(1-\eta)}{2K-5}}+\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-1-\Lambda}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\nu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{20}}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-1+\eta-\Lambda}\leq\nu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{10}}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-1+\eta-\Lambda}.

Therefore, it follows from Lemma
5.2
, (
5.19
), and (
5
) that

\displaystyle e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}e^{\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\big|\frac{\delta}{\alpha}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nabla(\partial_{y_{i}}Q)\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big|

(5.26)

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\bar{\tau}}\nu_{1}^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nu_{0}^{\frac{4}{5}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-1+2\eta+\delta_{\rm{dis}}-\Lambda}e^{(-1+2\eta)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}.

Moreover, when
1<\gamma<\frac{5}{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle-1+\delta_{\rm{dis}}

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}+1\big)(\Lambda-1)-2\leq\frac{2}{(1+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\gamma-1}})^{2}}\,\frac{2(1-\delta)+\gamma-1}{\gamma-1}-2

(5.27)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma+2-4\delta}{\gamma+1+2\sqrt{2(\gamma-1)}}-2<0.

When
\frac{5}{3}<\gamma<1+\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}
, we similarly have

\displaystyle-1+\delta_{\rm{dis}}

\displaystyle=\big(\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}+1\big)(\Lambda-1)-2\leq\frac{(3-\sqrt{3})(\gamma-1)+(6-2\sqrt{3})(1-\delta)}{2+\sqrt{3}(\gamma-1)}-2

(5.28)

\displaystyle=\frac{(3-3\sqrt{3})(\gamma-1)+(6-2\sqrt{3})(1-\delta)-4}{2+\sqrt{3}(\gamma-1)}<0.

Consequently, combining (
1.13
)–(
1.14
), (
5.21
)–(
5.24
), and (
5.26
)–(
5.28
), we choose
\eta\leq\frac{1}{10}(1-\delta_{\rm{dis}})
such that

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{y_{i}}U(y_{0}e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}},\tau)-e^{-\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\partial_{y_{i}}U(y_{0},\bar{\tau})|=e^{-\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\big|\omega_{U,y_{0},i}(\tau)-\omega_{U,y_{0},i}(\bar{\tau})|

(5.29)

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-2+2\eta}+Ce^{-\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\bar{\tau}}\nu_{1}^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\Lambda}\big(\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}}+\nu_{1}^{-1}\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}+\nu_{1}^{-2}\nu_{0}^{\frac{29}{10}}\big)

\displaystyle+Ce^{-\Lambda(\tau-\bar{\tau})}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\bar{\tau}}\nu_{1}^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nu_{0}^{\frac{4}{5}}\big\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\Lambda}

\displaystyle\leq\big\langle\frac{y_{0}e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\Lambda}.

Here we used the choice of
\eta
together with the fact that
\delta_{\rm dis}>0
and
\bar{\tau}\geq\tau_{0}

is sufficiently large to absorb all the large constants appearing in the above estimates.
For
U
, we also have

|\partial_{y_{i}}U(\bar{\tau},y_{0})|\leq C\qquad\,\text{for $|y_{0}|=R_{0}$ and $\bar{\tau}\geq\tau_{0}$},

and

|\partial_{y_{i}}U(\tau_{0},y_{0})|\leq C\langle\frac{y_{0}}{R_{0}}\rangle^{-\Lambda}\qquad\text{for $|y_{0}|\geq R_{0}$},

which, along with (
5.29
) and
y=y_{0}e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}
, yields that, for all
|y|\geq R_{0}
,

|\nabla U|\leq C\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\Lambda}.

(5.30)

Analogously to the proof of
\nabla\widetilde{Q}
, we obtain the decay of
\nabla\widetilde{U}
.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.3
.
∎

In addition, we derive estimates for the higher-order derivatives of
(Q,U)
.

Lemma 5.4
.

For every
1\leq j\leq K-2
and for all
(\tau,y)\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

|\nabla^{j}Q|+|\nabla^{j}U|\leq C(\nu_{0})\phi^{-\frac{j}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-(\Lambda-1)}.

(5.31)

For
j=K-1
,
\bar{\varepsilon}>0
, and for all
(\tau,y)\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\big\|\langle y\rangle^{K(1-\eta)\frac{K-2}{K-1}-\bar{\varepsilon}}|\nabla^{K-1}Q|\big\|_{L^{2+\frac{2}{K-2}}}+\big\|\langle y\rangle^{K(1-\eta)\frac{K-2}{K-1}-\bar{\varepsilon}}|\nabla^{K-1}U|\big\|_{L^{2+\frac{2}{K-2}}}\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon}).

(5.32)

Proof.

For (
5.31
), in the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.9
), choose

\varphi=\phi^{\frac{K}{2(K-1)}},\ \ \psi=\langle y\rangle^{\frac{\Lambda}{K-1}},\ \ l=K-1,\ \ i=j-1,\ \ p=\infty,\ \ q=2,\ \ \theta=\frac{j-1}{K-5/2}.

Then it follows from (
4.4
), (
4.21
), and (
5.30
) that

\displaystyle|\nabla^{j}U|

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\big(\|\langle y\rangle^{\Lambda}\nabla U\|^{1-\theta}_{L^{\infty}}\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}U\|^{\theta}_{L^{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-\Lambda(1-\theta)}\phi^{-\frac{K\theta}{2}}

(5.33)

\displaystyle+\|\langle y\rangle^{\Lambda}\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}}\langle y\rangle^{-j+1-\Lambda}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\big(R_{0}^{\Lambda}E^{\frac{\theta}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-\Lambda(1-\theta)}\phi^{-\frac{K}{2}\theta}+R_{0}^{\Lambda}\langle y\rangle^{-j+1-\Lambda}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\phi^{-\frac{j}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-(\Lambda-1)}\phi^{-\frac{K\theta}{2}+\frac{j}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-\Lambda(1-\theta)+\Lambda-1}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\phi^{-\frac{j}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-(\Lambda-1)}\phi^{\frac{K-5j/2}{2(K-5/2)}}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{\Lambda j-\Lambda-K+5/2}{K-5/2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\phi^{-\frac{j}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{-(\Lambda-1)}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{\Lambda j-\Lambda-K+5/2+(1-\eta)(K-5j/2)}{K-5/2}}.

For
1\leq j\leq K-2
, using
\frac{1}{K}\ll\eta\ll 1
yields

\Lambda j-\Lambda-K+\frac{5}{2}+(1-\eta)(K-\frac{5}{2}j)=(\Lambda-\frac{5}{2})(j-2)+\eta(\frac{5}{2}j-K)<0.

The estimate for
Q
is analogous. The proof of (
5.31
) is complete.

For (
5.32
), we use the assumption that
1\ll K\eta
,
together with the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.12
) applied to
\|\nabla U\langle y\rangle^{\Lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}U\|_{L^{2}}
. Choosing

\begin{split}&\varphi=\phi^{\frac{K}{2(K-1)}},\ \ \psi=\langle y\rangle^{\frac{\Lambda}{K-1}},\ \ l=K-1,\ \ i=K-2,\\
&\bar{r}=2+\frac{2}{K-2},\ \ p=\infty,\ \ q=2,\ \ \theta=\frac{K-2}{K-1},\end{split}

we obtain

\|\langle y\rangle^{-\bar{\varepsilon}+\frac{\Lambda}{K-1}+K(1-\eta)\frac{K-2}{K-1}}\nabla^{K-1}U\|_{L^{2+\frac{2}{K-2}}}\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon}).

(5.34)

The same bound holds for
Q
by an identical argument.
Therefore, (
5.32
) follows from
\Lambda>1
.
∎

6.
Global-In-Time Energy Estimates

This section is devoted to establishing refined estimates that strengthen the
a priori
assumptions in the bootstrap argument. These refinements allow us to close the bootstrap argument
and thereby obtain the desired global-in-time energy estimates of the solution.

6.1.
Proof of Proposition
4.1

In §
6.1
, the initial data function

(\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{U}_{0})
under consideration is constructed in (
4.10
)–(
4.13
),
1<\gamma<1+\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}
,
\Lambda\in(1,\Lambda^{*}(\gamma))
is introduced in Lemma
B.1
,
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
satisfies
\delta_{\rm dis}>0
,
and assumptions (
4.1
)–(
4.4
) and (
4.18
)–(
4.21
) always hold.

6.1.1.
Lower order temporal decay estimates

With the help of Lemmas
5.1
–
5.4
, we first obtain the
estimates for
\mathcal{E}
,
\mathcal{N}
, and
\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

For all
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
,

\displaystyle\chi_{2}\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}=0,\ \ \chi_{2}\mathcal{E}_{u}=0,

(6.1)

\displaystyle\max\big\{\|\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}\|_{L^{\infty}},\ \ \|\nabla^{4}\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big\}\leq\nu_{1}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})},

\displaystyle\max\big\{\|\mathcal{E}_{u}\|_{L^{\infty}},\ \ \|\nabla^{4}\mathcal{E}_{u}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big\}\leq\nu_{1}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Proof.

The decay of profile (
B.5
) gives

|\nabla^{j}\overline{Q}|+|\nabla^{j}\overline{U}|\leq C\langle y\rangle^{1-\Lambda-j}.

(6.2)

It follows from (
5.4
)–(
5.5
) that

\|(\widehat{X}^{2}-\widehat{X})\overline{U}\cdot\nabla\overline{U}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|(\widehat{X}^{2}-\widehat{X})\langle y\rangle^{1-2\Lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq Ce^{(1-2\Lambda)\tau},

and

\|\widehat{X}\overline{U}\cdot\nabla\widehat{X}\,\overline{U}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\||\widehat{X}||\nabla\widehat{X}|\langle y\rangle^{2-2\Lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq Ce^{(1-2\Lambda)\tau}.

All the remaining terms in
\mathcal{E}_{u}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}
can be estimated in a similar way.
Since
\Lambda>1
, and
\tau_{0}\gg\nu_{1}^{-1}\gg 1
, we deduce

\|\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathcal{E}_{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq Ce^{(1-2\Lambda)\tau}\leq Ce^{-\tau_{0}}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}\leq\frac{C}{\tau_{0}}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}\leq\nu_{1}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

We obtain from (
5.4
)–(
5.5
) and (
6.2
) that

\big|\nabla^{4}\big((\widehat{X}^{2}-\widehat{X})\overline{U}\cdot\nabla\overline{U}\big)\big|\leq C\sum_{0\leq j\leq 4}|\nabla^{4-j}(\widehat{X}^{2}-\widehat{X})||\nabla^{j}(\overline{U}\cdot\nabla\overline{U})|\leq C\sum_{0\leq j\leq 4}e^{-(4-j)\tau}\langle y\rangle^{1-2\Lambda-j}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla^{4}\big((\widehat{X}^{2}-\widehat{X})\overline{U}\cdot\nabla\overline{U}\big)\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{0\leq j\leq 4}e^{-2(4-j)\tau}\int_{\frac{1}{2}e^{\tau}}^{e^{\tau}}r^{2(1-2\Lambda-j)}r^{2}\text{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{0\leq j\leq 4}e^{(-2(4-j)+5-4\Lambda-2j)\tau}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{(-4\Lambda-3)\tau}\leq Ce^{-2\tau}.

The rest of the terms in
\nabla^{4}\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}
and
\nabla^{4}\mathcal{E}_{u}
can be estimated
in a similar way. We obtain from (
4.1
) and
\tau_{0}\gg 1
that

\|\nabla^{4}\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{4}\mathcal{E}_{u}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\leq Ce^{-\tau}\leq Ce^{-\tau_{0}}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}\leq\frac{C}{\tau_{0}}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}\leq\nu_{1}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

∎

Lemma 6.2
.

For all
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
,

\max\big\{\|\mathcal{N}_{\zeta}\|_{L^{\infty}},\ \|\chi_{2}\mathcal{N}_{\zeta}\|_{X},\ \|\mathcal{N}_{u}\|_{L^{\infty}},\ \|\chi_{2}\mathcal{N}_{u}\|_{X}\big\}\leq\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})},

(6.3)

and

\max\big\{\|\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}\|_{L^{\infty}},\ \|\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}\|_{X}\big\}\leq\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\frac{\tau}{2}}.

(6.4)

Proof.

First, using Lemmas
5.2
–
5.4
yields

\displaystyle e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\bigl|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}L(U)\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-2(1-\eta)-(\Lambda-1)+\frac{(1-\delta)(\Lambda-1)}{\alpha}}

(6.5)

\displaystyle=C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{\delta_{\rm{dis}}-2\Lambda+1+2\eta}\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau},

and

\displaystyle e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\big|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\frac{\nabla Q}{Q}\mathbb{D}(U)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-2+\eta-(\Lambda-1)+\frac{(1-\delta)(\Lambda-1)}{\alpha}}

(6.6)

\displaystyle=C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{\delta_{\rm{dis}}-2\Lambda+1+\eta}\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau},

where we have used the facts that
\delta_{\rm{dis}}-2\Lambda+1+2\eta<0
and
\eta>0
.

It follows from (
4.1
),
\tau\geq\tau_{0}\gg 1
, and (
6.5
)–(
6.6
) that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\big\|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}L(U)+Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\frac{\nabla Q}{Q}\mathbb{D}(U)\big\|_{L^{\infty}}

(6.7)

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\leq C(\nu_{0})\nu_{0}^{-\frac{9}{5}}e^{-\delta_{\rm dis}\frac{\tau}{2}}\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}e^{-\delta_{\rm dis}\frac{\tau}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm dis}\frac{\tau_{0}}{2}}\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\frac{\tau}{2}}\leq\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\frac{\tau}{2}}.

By the choice of
C_{0}
in Lemma
4.1
, the decay estimate of profile (
B.5
), (
4.19
), and
\nu_{0}\ll 1
, we have

Q\geq\overline{Q}-|\widetilde{Q}|\geq C^{-1}-\nu_{0}\geq C^{-1}\qquad\text{for $|y|\leq 3C_{0}$}.

Thus, we obtain from (
4.1
) and Lemmas
5.2
–
5.4
that

\displaystyle\|\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}\|_{H^{m}}

\displaystyle\leq C(m)\big(\|\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,3C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{m}\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,3C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(m)e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\big\|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}L(U)+Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\frac{\nabla Q}{Q}\mathbb{D}(U)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,3C_{0}))}

\displaystyle\quad+C(m)e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\big\|\nabla^{m}(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}L(U))+\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\nabla^{m}(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\frac{\nabla Q}{Q}\mathbb{D}(U))\big\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,3C_{0}))}

(6.8)

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\leq C(\nu_{0})\nu_{0}^{-\frac{9}{5}}e^{-\delta_{\rm dis}\frac{\tau}{2}}\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}e^{-\delta_{\rm dis}\frac{\tau}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm dis}\frac{\tau_{0}}{2}}\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\frac{\tau}{2}}\leq\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\frac{\tau}{2}}.

Next, we obtain from the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.10
), (
4.19
), and Lemma
5.1
that, for any integer
0\leq j\leq m
,

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla^{j}\big((\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{U}\big)\big\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C(m)\sum_{0\leq\ell\leq j\leq m}\|\nabla^{\ell}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{j-\ell+1}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C(m)E^{\frac{1}{20}}\nu_{0}^{\frac{39}{20}}e^{-\frac{39}{20}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq C(m)\nu_{0}^{\frac{19}{10}}e^{-\frac{39}{20}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

A similar estimate holds for
\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla\widetilde{Q}
,
\widetilde{Q}\nabla\widetilde{Q}
, and
\widetilde{Q}\,\mathrm{div}\widetilde{U}
:

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{j}(\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla\widetilde{Q})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla^{j}(\widetilde{Q}\nabla\widetilde{Q})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla^{j}(\widetilde{Q}\,\mathrm{div}\widetilde{U})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C(m)\sum_{0\leq\ell\leq j\leq m}\big(\|\nabla^{\ell}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{j-\ell+1}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla^{\ell}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{j-\ell+1}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(m)\sum_{0\leq\ell\leq j\leq m}\|\nabla^{\ell}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{j-\ell+1}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C(m)E^{\frac{1}{20}}\nu_{0}^{\frac{39}{20}}e^{-\frac{39}{20}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\leq C(m)\nu_{0}^{\frac{19}{10}}e^{-\frac{39}{20}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Consequently, it follows from (
4.1
) that

\|\mathcal{N}_{\zeta}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathcal{N}_{u}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(m)\nu_{0}^{\frac{19}{10}}e^{-\frac{39}{20}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\leq\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Moreover,
\text{supp}\,\chi_{2}\subset B(0,3C_{0})
and (
4.1
) imply

\|\chi_{2}\mathcal{N}_{u}\|_{H^{m}}\leq C(m)\sum_{j\leq m}\|\nabla^{j}(\mathcal{N}_{u})\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,3C_{o}))}\leq C(m)\nu_{0}^{\frac{19}{10}}e^{-\frac{39}{20}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\leq\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

\|\chi_{2}\mathcal{N}_{\zeta}\|_{H^{m}}
can be estimated analogously.

This completes the proof.
∎

We now perform the bootstrap argument. We first carry out the proof in region
B(0,C_{0})
.

Lemma 6.3
.

For any integer
0\leq j\leq 4
and all
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
,

\|\nabla^{j}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{j}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,C_{0}))}\leq C\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
4.2
and the definition of
X
in (
2.3
) that

\|\nabla^{j}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,C_{0}))}=\|\nabla^{j}\overset{\approx}{U}\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,C_{0}))}\leq C\|\overset{\approx}{U}\|_{X}.

Since
(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})
satisfies

\partial_{\tau}(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})=(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})+\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U})

with
\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}_{\zeta}=\chi_{2}\mathcal{N}_{\zeta}
and
\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}_{u}=\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}+\chi_{2}\mathcal{N}_{u}
, we project the equation onto the stable subspace to obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}P_{\rm{sta}}(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})=\mathcal{L}P_{\rm{sta}}(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})+P_{\rm{sta}}(\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U})).

Applying the Duhamel formula yields

\displaystyle P_{\rm{sta}}(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})(\tau)=\aleph(\tau-\tau_{0})P_{\rm{sta}}(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})(\tau_{0})+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\aleph(\tau-\bar{\tau})(P_{\rm{sta}}(\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U})))(\bar{\tau})\,{\rm d}\bar{\tau},

where
\aleph
is the semigroup generated by
\mathcal{L}
. Based on Lemmas
6.1
–
6.2
,
\nu_{g}=\frac{25}{12}\varepsilon
, and the fact that
\aleph
is a contraction semigroup in
V_{\rm{sta}}
determined in Lemma
C.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|P_{\rm{sta}}(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})\|_{X}

\displaystyle\leq\nu_{1}e^{-\frac{\nu_{g}}{2}(\tau-\tau_{0})}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}2\nu_{1}e^{-\frac{\nu_{g}}{2}(\tau-\bar{\tau})}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon(\bar{\tau}-\tau_{0})}\,{\rm d}\bar{\tau}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}e^{-\frac{\nu_{g}}{2}(\tau-\tau_{0})}\leq C\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

The unstable part of the solution is controlled directly from the assumption in Proposition
4.1
.
∎

Next, we derive the estimates in
B^{c}(0,C_{0})
.

Lemma 6.4
.

For
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
and
|y|\geq C_{0}
,

\max\big\{|\widetilde{Q}|,\ |\widetilde{U}|\big\}\leq\frac{{\nu_{0}}}{200}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Proof.

The perturbation
(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U})
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\widetilde{Q}

\displaystyle=-(\Lambda-1)\widetilde{Q}-y\cdot\nabla\widetilde{Q}-(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\cdot\nabla\widetilde{Q}-\alpha(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\,\mathrm{div}\,\widetilde{U}-\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})

\displaystyle-\alpha\widetilde{Q}\,\mathrm{div}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})+\mathcal{N}_{\zeta}+\mathcal{E}_{\zeta},

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\widetilde{U}

\displaystyle=-(\Lambda-1)\widetilde{U}-y\cdot\nabla\widetilde{U}-(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\cdot\nabla\widetilde{U}-\alpha(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\nabla\widetilde{Q}-\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U})

\displaystyle-\alpha\widetilde{Q}\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})+\mathcal{N}_{u}+\mathcal{E}_{u}+\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}.

Applying Lemma
A.4
between
\|(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U})\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\|(\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q},\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}
with

p=\infty,\quad q=2,\quad\theta=\frac{2}{5},\quad\bar{r}=\infty,\quad i=1,\quad l=4,

we obtain

\max\big\{\|\nabla\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))},\ \|\nabla\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big\}\leq\nu_{0}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Combining this bound with (
4.5
) and (
4.19
) yields

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{X}\overline{U}\cdot\nabla\widetilde{Q}\big|+\big|\alpha(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\,\mathrm{div}\widetilde{U}\big|+\big|\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\big|+\big|\alpha\widetilde{Q}\,\mathrm{div}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\big|\leq\nu_{0}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})},

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{X}\overline{U}\cdot\nabla\widetilde{U}\big|+\big|\alpha(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\nabla\widetilde{Q}\big|+\big|\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\big|+\big|\alpha\widetilde{Q}\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\big|\leq\nu_{0}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Then, according to Lemmas
6.1
–
6.2
, for
1\leq i\leq 3
and
\tau_{0}\leq\bar{\tau}\leq\tau
, we have

\begin{split}&|\partial_{\tau}\widetilde{U}_{i}(e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}y_{0})+(\Lambda-1)\widetilde{U}_{i}(e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}y_{0})|\\
&\leq\nu_{0}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}+\nu_{1}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}+\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\frac{3\varepsilon}{2}(\tau-\tau_{0})}+\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\frac{\tau}{2}},\end{split}

(6.9)

and

\begin{split}&|\partial_{\tau}\widetilde{Q}(e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}y_{0})+(\Lambda-1)\widetilde{Q}(e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}y_{0})|\\
&\leq\nu_{0}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}+\nu_{1}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}+\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\frac{3\varepsilon}{2}(\tau-\tau_{0})}.\end{split}

(6.10)

It follows from (
6.9
),
\nu_{g}\ll\delta_{\rm dis}
, and
\varepsilon=\frac{12\nu_{g}}{25}
that

\displaystyle|\partial_{\tau}\big(\widetilde{U}_{i}(e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}y_{0})e^{\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\big)+(\Lambda-1-\varepsilon)\widetilde{U}_{i}(e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}y_{0})e^{\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}|

\displaystyle=|e^{\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\big(\partial_{\tau}\widetilde{U}_{i}(e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}y_{0})+(\Lambda-1)\widetilde{U}_{i}(e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}y_{0})\big)|

\displaystyle\leq\nu_{0}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}+\nu_{1}e^{-(1-\varepsilon)(\tau-\tau_{0})}+\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}(\tau-\tau_{0})}+\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-(\delta_{\rm{dis}}\frac{\tau}{2}-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq 2\nu_{0}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}.

Multiplying the above by
e^{(\Lambda-1-\varepsilon)(\tau-\tau_{0})}
leads to

|\partial_{\tau}\big(\widetilde{U}_{i}(e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}y_{0})e^{(\Lambda-1)(\tau-\tau_{0})}\big)|\leq 2\nu_{0}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}e^{(\Lambda-1-\varepsilon)(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

(6.11)

Integrating (
6.11
) over
[\bar{\tau},\tau]
and then multiplying by
e^{-(\Lambda-1-\varepsilon)(\tau-\tau_{0})}
yield

\displaystyle|\widetilde{U}_{i}(e^{\tau-\bar{\tau}}y_{0},\tau)e^{\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}|

\displaystyle\leq|\widetilde{U}_{i}(y_{0},\bar{\tau})e^{(\Lambda-1)(\bar{\tau}-\tau_{0})}e^{-(\Lambda-1-\varepsilon)(\tau-\tau_{0})}|+\frac{2\nu_{0}}{\Lambda-1-\varepsilon}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}(1-e^{-(\Lambda-1-\varepsilon)(\tau-\bar{\tau})})

\displaystyle\leq|\widetilde{U}_{i}(y_{0},\bar{\tau})e^{-(\Lambda-1-\varepsilon)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}e^{\varepsilon(\bar{\tau}-\tau_{0})}|+\frac{8\nu_{0}}{\Lambda-1}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}(1-e^{-(\Lambda-1-\varepsilon)(\tau-\bar{\tau})})

\displaystyle=e^{-(\Lambda-1-\varepsilon)(\tau-\bar{\tau})}\Big(|\widetilde{U}_{i}(y_{0},\bar{\tau})e^{\varepsilon(\bar{\tau}-\tau_{0})}|-\frac{8\nu_{0}}{\Lambda-1}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}\Big)+\frac{8\nu_{0}}{\Lambda-1}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}.

For
|y_{0}|=C_{0}
and
\bar{\tau}\geq\tau_{0}
, using Lemma
6.3
and
\nu_{1}\ll\nu_{0}\ll\nu_{g}
, we have

\max\big\{|\widetilde{Q}(y_{0},\bar{\tau})e^{\varepsilon(\bar{\tau}-\tau_{0})}|,\ |\widetilde{U}_{i}(y_{0},\bar{\tau})e^{\varepsilon(\bar{\tau}-\tau_{0})}|\big\}\leq C\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\leq\frac{8\nu_{0}}{\Lambda-1}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}.

For
|y_{0}|>C_{0}
and
\bar{\tau}=\tau_{0}
, according to the initial condition (
4.12
) and
\nu_{1}\ll\nu_{0}
, we have

\max\big\{|\widetilde{Q}(y_{0},\tau_{0})|,\ |\widetilde{U}_{i}(y_{0},\tau_{0})|\big\}\leq\nu_{1}\leq\frac{8\nu_{0}}{\Lambda-1}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}.

Then the upper bound of
\widetilde{U}
follows from the chosen constant parameters (
4.9
):

\frac{32}{\Lambda-1}\frac{1}{C_{1}}\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{20}}=\frac{1}{100}.

The upper bound of
\widetilde{Q}
can be proved by a similar way.

This completes the proof.
∎

Now we control
\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}
and
\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}
.

Lemma 6.5
.

For
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
,

\max\big\{\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))},\ \|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big\}\leq\frac{\nu_{0}}{2}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Proof.

We divide the proof into four steps.

1.

First, applying the fourth-order derivative
\partial_{\beta}
with multi-index
\beta=(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3},\beta_{4})
satisfying
|\beta|=4
to (
2.18
)–(
2.19
), we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}

\displaystyle=B_{\zeta,0}(\widetilde{U},\widetilde{Q})+B_{\zeta,1}(\widetilde{U},\widetilde{Q})+B_{\zeta,2}(\widetilde{U},\widetilde{Q})+B_{\zeta,3}(\widetilde{U},\widetilde{Q})+\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{N}_{\zeta}+\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{E}_{\zeta},

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}

\displaystyle=B_{u,0}(\widetilde{U},\widetilde{Q})+B_{u,1}(\widetilde{U},\widetilde{Q})+B_{u,2}(\widetilde{U},\widetilde{Q})+B_{u,3}(\widetilde{U},\widetilde{Q})+\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{N}_{u}+\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{E}_{u}+\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}.

Here
B_{\zeta,0}
and
B_{u,0}
collect the linear terms with the fifth-order derivatives
fall on
\widetilde{Q}
and
\widetilde{U}
.
The terms
B_{\zeta,1}
and
B_{u,1}
consist of the linear terms
with the fourth-order derivatives acting on
\widetilde{Q}
and
\widetilde{U}
.
Similarly,
B_{\zeta,2}
and
B_{u,2}
contain the linear terms with the third-order derivatives,
while
B_{\zeta,3}
and
B_{u,3}
comprise the linear terms with less than or equal
to the second-order derivatives acting on
\widetilde{Q}
and
\widetilde{U}
.

Multiplying the above equations by
\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}
and

\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}
respectively, summing over all

|\beta|=4
, and then integrating over
B^{c}(0,C_{0})
, we obtain

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\sum_{|\beta|=4}\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\beta|=4}\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle=2\sum_{|\beta|=4}\sum_{j=0}^{3}\bigg(\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot B_{u,j}\,\text{d}y+\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\,B_{\zeta,j}\,\text{d}y\bigg)

(6.12)

\displaystyle\quad+2\sum_{|\beta|=4}\bigg(\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{N}_{u}\,\text{d}y+\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\,\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{N}_{\zeta}\,\text{d}y\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+2\sum_{|\beta|=4}\bigg(\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{E}_{u}\,\text{d}y+\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\,\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}\,\text{d}y\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+2\sum_{|\beta|=4}\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle:=\sum_{|\beta|=4}\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{3}I_{j,\beta}+I_{N,\beta}+I_{E,\beta}+I_{F,\beta}\Big).

2.

For
I_{0,\beta}
, it follows from (
4.17
) and integration by parts that

\displaystyle I_{0,\beta}

\displaystyle=-2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\Big(\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\big((y+\widehat{X}\overline{U})\cdot\nabla\big)\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}+(\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla)\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}(\alpha\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\Big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\Big(\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}(y+\widehat{X}\overline{U})\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}+\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\,\mathrm{div}(\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U})\alpha\widehat{X}\overline{Q}\Big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\Big(3+|\mathrm{div}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})|\Big)\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}|\nabla(\alpha\widehat{X}\overline{Q})||\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}||\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\partial B(0,C_{0})}\big(C_{0}+|\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}|\big)\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}S+2\int_{\partial B(0,C_{0})}|\alpha\widehat{X}\overline{Q}||\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}||\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|\,\text{d}S.

By (
4.1
), (
B.5
), Lemma
6.3
, and the smoothness of
(\widetilde{Q},\widetilde{U})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B(0,C_{0})}\big(C_{0}+|\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}|\big)\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}S+2\int_{\partial B(0,C_{0})}|\alpha\widehat{X}\overline{Q}||\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}||\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|\,\text{d}S

\displaystyle\leq C(C_{0}+|\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}|+\alpha|\widehat{X}\overline{Q}|)\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{2}e^{-\nu_{g}(\tau-\tau_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}e^{-2\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle I_{0,\beta}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\big(3+3|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U})|+|\nabla(\alpha\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|\big)\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y+\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}e^{-2\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

(6.13)

Next,
B_{\zeta,1}
and
B_{u,1}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle B_{\zeta,1}
