\displaystyle=-(\Lambda-1)\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}-(\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla)(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})-\alpha\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\,\mathrm{div}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{4}\sum_{l=1}^{3}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}(\widehat{X}\overline{U}_{l}+y_{l})\partial_{y_{l}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}\widetilde{Q}-\alpha\sum_{j=1}^{4}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}\mathrm{div}\,\widetilde{U}

\displaystyle=-(\Lambda+3)\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}-(\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla)(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})-\alpha\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\,\mathrm{div}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{4}\sum_{l=1}^{3}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}(\widehat{X}\overline{U}_{l})\partial_{y_{l}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}\widetilde{Q}-\alpha\sum_{j=1}^{4}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}\mathrm{div}\,\widetilde{U},

and

\displaystyle B_{u,1}

\displaystyle=-(\Lambda-1)\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}-(\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla)(\widehat{X}\overline{U})-\alpha\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{4}\sum_{l=1}^{3}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}(\widehat{X}\overline{U}_{l}+y_{l})\partial_{y_{l}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}\widetilde{U}-\alpha\sum_{j=1}^{4}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\nabla(\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}\widetilde{Q})

\displaystyle=-(\Lambda+3)\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}-(\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla)(\widehat{X}\overline{U})-\alpha\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{4}\sum_{l=1}^{3}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}(\widehat{X}\overline{U}_{l})\partial_{y_{l}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}\widetilde{U}-\alpha\sum_{j=1}^{4}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\nabla(\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}\widetilde{Q}),

where the differential operator
\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}
is defined in §
2.1.1
. Since

\alpha=\frac{\gamma-1}{2},\quad 1<\gamma<1+\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}},

we see that
0<\alpha<\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
.
Then it follows that

\displaystyle I_{1,\beta}

\displaystyle=2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}B_{\zeta,1}\,\text{d}y+2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot B_{u,1}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq-2(\Lambda+3)\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

(6.14)

\displaystyle\quad+20\big(\|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,\,\times\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}\big).

For
B_{u,2}
and
B_{\zeta,2}
, according to (
4.8
), the Hölder inequality.
and Lemma
A.4
with

p=\infty,\quad q=2,\quad\theta=\frac{3}{4},\quad\bar{r}=\frac{8}{3},\quad i=3,\quad l=4,

we derive

\displaystyle\|B_{\zeta,2}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|B_{u,2}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq 80\big(\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{8}({B^{c}(0,C_{0})})}+\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{8}({B^{c}(0,C_{0})})}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,\,\,\times\big(\|\nabla^{3}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{3}{\widetilde{Q}}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 80\big(\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{8}({B^{c}(0,C_{0})})}+\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{8}({B^{c}(0,C_{0})})}\big)\nu_{0}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle I_{2,\beta}

\displaystyle=2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot B_{u,2}\,\text{d}y+2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\,B_{\zeta,2}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq 160\big(\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{8}({B^{c}(0,C_{0})})}+\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{8}({B^{c}(0,C_{0})})}\big)

(6.15)

\displaystyle\quad\,\times\Big(\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}\big)\text{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{0}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

For
B_{u,3}
and
B_{\zeta,3}
, we employ (
4.8
), (
4.19
)–(
4.20
), and Lemma
A.4
with

p=\infty,\quad q=2,\quad\theta=\frac{4}{5},\quad\bar{r}=\infty,\quad i=2,\quad l=4,

and

p=\infty,\quad q=2,\quad\theta=\frac{2}{5},\quad\bar{r}=\infty,\quad i=1,\quad l=4,

to obtain

\displaystyle\|B_{\zeta,3}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|B_{u,3}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq 80\big(\|\widetilde{U}\|_{W^{2,\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\widetilde{Q}\|_{W^{2,\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,\,\times\sum_{j=3}^{5}\big(\|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 80\sum_{j=3}^{5}\big(\|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)\nu_{0}\,100^{-\frac{1}{20}}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})},

which implies

\displaystyle I_{3,\beta}

\displaystyle=2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot B_{u,3}\,\text{d}y+2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\,B_{\zeta,3}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq 160\sum_{j=3}^{5}\big(\|\nabla^{j}\widehat{X}\overline{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

(6.16)

\displaystyle\quad\,\,\times\Big(\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}\big)\text{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{0}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

3.
For the nonlinear terms
(\mathcal{N}_{\zeta},\mathcal{N}_{u})
, it follows from (
4.1
), (
4.19
)–(
4.20
), and Lemmas
5.1
and
A.4
that

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{8}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{\frac{7}{20}}\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{\frac{13}{20}}+\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\nu_{0}\Big(\frac{1}{100}+\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{7}{20}}\Big)e^{\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{3}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{\frac{3}{4}}+\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\nu_{0}\Big(\frac{1}{100}+\big(\frac{1}{100}\big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big)e^{\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{5}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{\frac{K-5}{K-4}}\|\nabla^{K}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{\frac{1}{K-4}}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(K)(E^{\frac{1}{10}}\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}}e^{-\frac{9}{10}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}+\nu_{0}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})})\leq C(K)\nu_{0}^{\frac{4}{5}}e^{-\frac{9}{10}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

The corresponding estimates for
\widetilde{Q}
follow analogously. Therefore, we obtain from
\nu_{0}\ll\frac{1}{K}
that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{N}_{\zeta}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{N}_{u}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla^{5}\widetilde{U}\|_{{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}+\|\nabla^{5}\widetilde{Q}\|_{{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}\big)\big(\|\widetilde{U}\|_{{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}+\|\widetilde{Q}\|_{{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}\big)\big(\|\nabla\widetilde{U}\|_{{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}+\|\nabla\widetilde{Q}\|_{{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\|\nabla^{3}\widetilde{U}\|_{{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}+\|\nabla^{3}\widetilde{Q}\|_{{L^{\frac{8}{3}}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}\big)\big(\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{U}\|_{{L^{8}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}+\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{Q}\|_{{L^{8}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{5}}e^{-\frac{19}{10}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\leq\nu_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle I_{N,\beta}

\displaystyle=2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{N}_{u}\,\text{d}y+2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\,\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{N}_{\zeta}\,\text{d}y

(6.17)

\displaystyle\leq 2{\nu_{0}}^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\Big(\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For terms
\mathcal{E}_{u}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}
, Lemma

6.1
gives

\displaystyle I_{E,\beta}

\displaystyle=2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{E}_{u}\,\text{d}y+2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}\,\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}\,\text{d}y

(6.18)

\displaystyle\leq 4\nu_{1}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}\Big(\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Before estimating the diffusion term
\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}
,
we first apply (
5.33
) to derive the following estimate:

\displaystyle\left|\nabla^{j}(L(U))\right|

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},j)\langle y\rangle^{-\frac{K-(j+2)-3/2+K(1-\eta)(j+2-1)}{K-5/2}}=C(\nu_{0},j)\langle y\rangle^{-\frac{K-j-7/2+K(1-\eta)(j+1)}{K-5/2}}.

Moreover, for any integer
\ell\in[1,j]
and integers
k_{i}\geq 1
(
1\leq i\leq\ell
) satisfying
k_{1}+\cdots+k_{\ell}=j
, it follows from Lemmas
5.2
–
5.4
and
\nu_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}\ll\nu_{1}\ll\nu_{0}
that

\displaystyle\big|\nabla^{j}(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C(j)\sum_{k_{1}+\cdots+k_{\ell}=j}\frac{|\nabla^{k_{1}}Q||\nabla^{k_{2}}Q|\cdots|\nabla^{k_{\ell}}Q|}{Q^{\ell}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},j)\sum_{k_{1}+\cdots+k_{\ell}=j}\nu_{1}^{-\ell+\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\phi^{-\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}+\cdots+k_{\ell}}{2}}\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},j)\phi^{-\frac{j}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)}.

Combining the above two estimates with (
1.13
)–(
1.14
) and (
2.14
) yields

\displaystyle\big|\partial^{\beta}(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}L(U))\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{0\leq j\leq 4}\big|\nabla^{4-j}(L(U))\big|\big|\nabla^{j}(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}})\big|

(6.19)

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\sum_{0\leq j\leq 4}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)-j(1-\eta)-\frac{K+j-15/2+K(1-\eta)(5-j)}{K-5/2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\langle y\rangle^{-5(1-\eta)+2-\Lambda+\delta_{\rm{dis}}}\leq C(\nu_{0})\langle y\rangle^{-3+5\eta},

and

\displaystyle\big|\partial^{\beta}(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nabla Q\mathbb{D}(U))\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{0\leq j\leq 4}\big|\nabla^{4-j}(\mathbb{D}(U))\big|\big|\nabla^{j}(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nabla Q)\big|

(6.20)

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\sum_{0\leq j\leq 4}\big|\nabla^{4-j}(\mathbb{D}(U))\big|\phi^{-\frac{j+1}{2}}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\sum_{0\leq j\leq 4}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)-(j+1)(1-\eta)-\frac{K+j-13/2+K(1-\eta)(4-j)}{K-5/2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\langle y\rangle^{-5(1-\eta)+2-\Lambda+\delta_{\rm{dis}}}\leq C(\nu_{0})\langle y\rangle^{-3+5\eta}.

Using (
4.1
) and (
6.19
)–(
6.20
) and denoting
r=|y|
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\beta}\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\Big(\int_{C_{0}}^{\infty}\langle r\rangle^{-6+10\eta}r^{2}\text{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\nu_{0}^{-\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\delta_{\rm dis}\tau_{0}}\nu_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\delta_{\rm dis}(\tau-\tau_{0})}\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm dis}\tau_{0}}\nu_{1}e^{-\nu_{g}(\tau-\tau_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq\nu_{1}e^{-\nu_{g}(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle I_{F,\beta}

\displaystyle=2\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}\cdot\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}\,\text{d}y

(6.21)

\displaystyle\leq 2\nu_{1}e^{-\nu_{g}(\tau-\tau_{0})}\Big(\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

4.

Gathering all the above bounds together and using
0<\alpha<\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle I\leq

\displaystyle\,\Big(-2\Lambda-3+3|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U})|+|\nabla(\alpha\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|\Big)\Big(\sum_{|\beta|=4}\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y\Big)

\displaystyle+81\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}e^{-2\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}

\displaystyle+1620\big(\|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,\,\times\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{|\beta|=4}\Big(\int_{B^{c}(0,C_{0})}\big(|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{U}|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{Q}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\,\,\times\Big(160\big(\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{8}({B^{c}(0,C_{0})})}+\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{8}({B^{c}(0,C_{0})})}\big)\Big.\nu_{0}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\,+160\sum_{j=3}^{5}\big(\|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)\nu_{0}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\,+\Big.2{\nu_{0}}^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}+4\nu_{1}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}+2\nu_{1}e^{-\nu_{g}(\tau-\tau_{0})}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\Big(-3-2\Lambda+2000\|(\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U}),\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q}))\|_{L^{\infty}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\Big)

\displaystyle\,\,\times\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}\big)+81\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}e^{-2\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}

\displaystyle+160\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\,\,\times\Big(\big(\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{8}({B^{c}(0,C_{0})})}+\|\nabla^{2}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{8}({B^{c}(0,C_{0})})}\big)\Big.\nu_{0}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,\,\,\,+\sum_{j=3}^{5}\big(\|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)\nu_{0}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}

\displaystyle\qquad\,\,\,\,\,+\Big.2{\nu_{0}}^{\frac{3}{2}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}+4\nu_{1}e^{-(\tau-\tau_{0})}+2\nu_{1}e^{-\nu_{g}(\tau-\tau_{0})}\Big).

It follows from (
4.6
)–(
4.8
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq-\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}\big)+81\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}e^{-2\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{20}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}\nu_{0}e^{-\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For convenience, denote
A(\tau)=\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}\big)e^{2\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}A(\tau)\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}A(\tau)+\frac{\nu_{0}}{20}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}\sqrt{A(\tau)}+81\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{8}A(\tau)+\Big(\frac{\nu_{0}}{10}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}\Big)^{2}+81\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}.

Then applying the Grönwall inequality yields

\displaystyle A(\tau)

\displaystyle\leq A(\tau_{0})e^{-\frac{3}{8}(\tau-\tau_{0})}+\frac{8}{3}\bigg(\Big(\frac{\nu_{0}}{10}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}\Big)^{2}+81\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}\bigg)\big(1-e^{-\frac{3}{8}(\tau-\tau_{0})}\big)

\displaystyle\leq A(\tau_{0})e^{-\frac{3}{8}(\tau-\tau_{0})}+3\bigg(\Big(\frac{\nu_{0}}{10}100^{-\frac{1}{20}}\Big)^{2}+81\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}\bigg)\big(1-e^{-\frac{3}{8}(\tau-\tau_{0})}\big)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{3}{8}(\tau-\tau_{0})}\bigg(A(\tau_{0})-\Big(\frac{3\nu_{0}^{2}}{100}100^{-\frac{1}{10}}+243\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}\Big)\bigg)+\Big(\frac{3\nu_{0}^{2}}{100}100^{-\frac{1}{10}}+243\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}\Big).

Using (
4.1
) and the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.14
) between
\|(\widetilde{U}_{0},\widetilde{Q}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\widetilde{E}_{K}
, we have

\begin{split}A(\tau_{0})&=\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}\leq C(K)(\nu_{1}^{\frac{2K-8}{K-3/2}}E^{\frac{5}{K-3/2}}+\nu_{1}^{2})\\
&\leq\nu_{1}^{\frac{9}{5}}\leq\frac{3\nu_{0}^{2}}{100}100^{-\frac{1}{10}}+243\big(\frac{\nu_{1}}{\nu_{g}}\big)^{\frac{17}{10}}\leq\frac{1}{4}\nu_{0}^{2}.\end{split}

Therefore, we conclude

\Big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}\Big)e^{2\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\leq\frac{1}{4}\nu_{0}^{2}.

(6.22)

This completes the proof of Lemma
6.5
.
∎

6.1.2.
Higher-order weighted energy estimates

Next, we establish the bootstrap estimate for the higher-order weighted energy
E_{K}
.
For the higher–order weighted energy estimates, we first derive the following commutator estimates for the
K
-th order spatial derivatives
of the nonlinear convection term.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
\beta=(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\cdots,\beta_{K})
be a multi-index with

\beta_{i}\in\{1,2,3\}
for
i=1,\cdots,K
,
and let
\beta^{(j)}
,
1\leq j\leq K
,
be the
\beta
–related multi-index as defined in (
2.1
).
Then, for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla U_{i})-U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}U_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{k=1}^{3}L_{k\beta_{j}}\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\Big)\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\,\,\leq E^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{4K}}+C\|\partial_{\beta}U\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}},

(6.23)

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big(\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla Q)-U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}Q-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{k=1}^{3}L_{k\beta_{j}}\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}Q\Big)\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\,\,\leq E^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{4K}}+C\|\partial_{\beta}Q\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}},

(6.24)

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(\partial_{\beta}(Q\partial_{y_{i}}Q)-Q\partial_{\beta}\partial_{y_{i}}Q-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\big(\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\frac{y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|}\big)\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}Q\Big)\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\,\,\leq E^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{4K}}+C\|\partial_{\beta}Q\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}},

(6.25)

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big(\partial_{\beta}(Q\,\mathrm{div}\,U)-Q\partial_{\beta}\,\mathrm{div}\,U-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\big(\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\frac{y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|}\big)\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}(\mathrm{div}U)\Big)\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\,\,\leq E^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{4K}}+C\|\partial_{\beta}U\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}},

(6.26)

where
L_{k\beta_{j}}
is given by

L_{k\beta_{j}}:=\big(\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}})-\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|}\big)\frac{y_{k}y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|^{2}}+\delta_{\beta_{j},k}\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|}.

Proof.

Since all those estimates are analogous, we just show (
6.23
).
Using the Leibniz rule to
\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla U_{i})
yields that, for any integers
2\leq\ell,\ell^{*}\leq K-1
satisfying
\ell+\ell^{*}=K+1
,

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla U_{i})-U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}U_{i}-\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\nabla U_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\big|

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq\ell,\ \ell^{*}\leq K-1,\\
\ \ell+\ell^{*}=K+1\end{subarray}}|\nabla^{\ell}U||\nabla^{\ell^{*}}U|.

To control the terms on the right-hand side of the above, we apply Lemma
A.3
with

\varphi=\phi^{\frac{1}{2}},\quad\psi=1,\quad p=\infty,\quad q=2,\quad l=K,\quad\bar{r}=\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell},\quad\theta=\frac{\ell-3/\bar{r}}{K-3/2},

together with (
1.13
)–(
1.14
), (
4.19
), and Lemma
5.1
, to obtain

\|\langle y\rangle^{\frac{1-\Lambda}{4(K+1)}}\phi^{\frac{K\theta}{2}}\nabla^{\ell}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\bar{r}}}\leq C(K)\big(\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{1-\theta}\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}}^{\theta}+\|\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\leq C(K)\nu_{0}^{1-\theta}E^{\frac{\theta}{2}}.

(6.27)

It follows from (
B.5
) and (
5.4
)–(
5.5
) that

\displaystyle|\nabla^{\ell}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})|

\displaystyle\leq|\widehat{X}\nabla^{\ell}(\overline{U})|+\sum_{1\leq j\leq\ell}|\nabla^{j}(\widehat{X})\nabla^{\ell-j}(\overline{U})|

\displaystyle\leq C\langle y\rangle^{-(\Lambda-1)-\ell}\mathbf{1}_{\{|y|\leq e^{\tau}\}}+C\sum_{1\leq j\leq\ell}e^{-j\tau}|y|^{-(\Lambda-1)-(\ell-j)}\mathbf{1}_{\{\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\leq|y|\leq e^{\tau}\}},

where

\mathbf{1}_{\{|y|\leq e^{\tau}\}}=\begin{cases}1&\mbox{ for $|y|\leq e^{\tau}$},\\[6.0pt]
0&\mbox{ for $|y|>e^{\tau}$},\end{cases}\qquad\mathbf{1}_{\{\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\leq|y|\leq e^{\tau}\}}=\begin{cases}1&\mbox{ for $\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}\leq|y|\leq e^{\tau}$},\\[6.0pt]
0&\mbox{ for $|y|<\frac{e^{\tau}}{2}$ or $|y|>e^{\tau}$}.\end{cases}

Consequently, it follows from
K\eta\gg 1
that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\|\phi^{\frac{K\theta}{2}}\nabla^{\ell}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|^{\bar{r}}_{L^{\bar{r}}}

(6.28)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{e^{\tau}}\phi^{\frac{K\theta}{2}\bar{r}}\langle r\rangle^{(-(\Lambda-1)-\ell)\bar{r}}r^{2}\,\text{d}r

\displaystyle+C\sum_{1\leq j\leq\ell}e^{-j\tau\bar{r}}\int_{e^{\tau}/2}^{e^{\tau}}\phi^{\frac{K\theta}{2}\bar{r}}r^{(-(\Lambda-1)-(\ell-j))\bar{r}}r^{2}\,\text{d}r

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{e^{\tau}}\langle r\rangle^{-(\Lambda-1)\bar{r}-2K\eta+\frac{2K-2K\eta}{K-3/2}}\,\text{d}r+C(K)e^{(1-(\Lambda-1)\bar{r}-2K\eta+\frac{2K-2K\eta}{K-3/2})\tau}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{e^{\tau}}\langle r\rangle^{-(\Lambda-1)\bar{r}-1}\text{d}r+C(K)e^{-(\Lambda-1)\bar{r}\tau}\leq C(K).

We obtain from (
4.1
), (
4.4
), (
6.27
), and the Hölder inequality that, for any integers
2\leq\ell,\ell^{*}\leq K-1
satisfying
\ell+\ell^{*}=K+1

and for
r^{*}=\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell^{*}}
,

\displaystyle\big\||\nabla^{\ell}\widetilde{U}||\nabla^{\ell^{*}}\widetilde{U}|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\big\||\nabla^{\ell}\widetilde{U}||\nabla^{\ell^{*}}\widetilde{U}|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}\frac{\ell-3/{\bar{r}}}{K-3/2}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}\frac{\ell^{*}-3/{r^{*}}}{K-3/2}}\phi^{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{K}{K-3/2}}\big\|_{L^{2}}

(6.29)

\displaystyle\leq C(K)R_{0}^{\frac{\Lambda-1}{2(K+1)}}\big\|\langle y\rangle^{\frac{1-\Lambda}{4(K+1)}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}\frac{\ell-3/{\bar{r}}}{K-3/2}}\nabla^{\ell}\widetilde{U}\big\|_{L^{\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell}}}\big\|\langle y\rangle^{\frac{1-\Lambda}{4(K+1)}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}\frac{\ell^{*}-3/{r^{*}}}{K-3/2}}\nabla^{\ell^{*}}\widetilde{U}\big\|_{L^{\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell^{*}}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\nu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2(K+1)}}\nu_{0}^{\frac{K-5/2}{K-3/2}}E^{\frac{K-1/2}{2K-3}}\leq C(K)\nu_{0}^{\frac{K-9/2}{2K-3}-\frac{1}{2(K+1)}}\leq C(K).

Similarly, combining (
4.1
), (
4.4
), and (
6.27
)–(
6.28
) leads to

\displaystyle\big\||\nabla^{\ell}\widetilde{U}||\nabla^{\ell^{*}}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)R_{0}^{\frac{\Lambda-1}{4(K+1)}}\big\|\langle y\rangle^{\frac{1-\Lambda}{4(K+1)}}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}\frac{\ell-3/{\bar{r}}}{K-3/2}}\nabla^{\ell}\widetilde{U}\big\|_{L^{\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell}}}\big\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}\frac{\ell^{*}-3/{r^{*}}}{K-3/2}}\nabla^{\ell^{*}}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\big\|_{L^{\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell^{*}}}}

(6.30)

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\nu_{0}^{-\frac{1}{4(K+1)}}\nu_{0}^{\frac{K-2}{(K-3/2)(K+1)}}E^{\frac{1}{2}\big(1-\frac{K-2}{(K-3/2)(K+1)}\big)}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\nu_{0}^{\frac{K-3}{(2K-3)(K+1)}-\frac{1}{4(K+1)}}E^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{K+1}}\leq C(K)E^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{K+1}},

and

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\big\||\nabla^{\ell}(\widehat{X}\overline{U}))||\nabla^{\ell^{*}}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\big\|_{L^{2}}

(6.31)

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\big\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}\frac{\ell-3/{\bar{r}}}{K-3/2}}\nabla^{\ell}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\big\|_{L^{\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell}}}\big\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}\frac{\ell^{*}-3/{r^{*}}}{K-3/2}}\nabla^{\ell^{*}}(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\big\|_{L^{\frac{2(K+1)}{\ell^{*}}}}\leq C(K).

We obtain from (
6.1.2
)–(
6.31
) and
E\gg 1
that

\big\||\nabla^{\ell}U||\nabla^{\ell^{*}}U|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\big\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(K)E^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{K+1}}.

(6.32)

Since
E
is sufficiently large and depends on
K
, we have

\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}\big(\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla U_{i})-U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}U_{i}-\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\nabla U_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\big)\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}\leq E^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2K}}.

(6.33)

On the other hand, applying (
4.1
) and the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.12
) between (
4.19
) and (
5.1
) gives

\|\nabla\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(K)\big(\nu_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{K-3/2}}E^{\frac{1}{2K-3}}+\nu_{0}\big)\leq\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}},

(6.34)

hence

\|\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|\nabla\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C.

(6.35)

This implies

\|\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\nabla U_{i}\,\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|\nabla U\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{\beta}U\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\partial_{\beta}U\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}},

(6.36)

and

\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{K}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}\widetilde{U}\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\,\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(K)\nu_{0}^{\frac{9}{10}}E^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\nu_{0}^{\frac{2}{5}}.

(6.37)

Finally, using that
\overline{U}
is spherically symmetric (
i.e.
,
\overline{U}=\overline{\mathcal{U}}\frac{y}{|y|}
),
for each
k\in\{1,2,3\}
, we decompose

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}U_{k}

\displaystyle=\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}\widetilde{U}_{k}+\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}(\widehat{X}\overline{U}_{k})=\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}\widetilde{U}_{k}+\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}\big(\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}\,\frac{y_{k}}{|y|}\big)

(6.38)

\displaystyle=\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}\widetilde{U}_{k}+\underbrace{\big(\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}})-\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|}\big)\frac{y_{k}y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|^{2}}+\delta_{\beta_{j},k}\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|}}_{L_{k\beta_{j}}}.

Combining (
6.33
) and (
6.36
)–(
6.38
) and using
\nu_{0}\ll\frac{1}{E}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla U_{i})-U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}U_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{k=1}^{3}L_{k\beta_{j}}\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\Big)\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

(6.39)

\displaystyle\leq E^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{4K}}+C\|\partial_{\beta}U\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}.

This completes the proof of Lemma
6.6
.
∎

With the above commutator estimates, we can close the
K
-th order weighted energy estimates under the
a priori
bootstrap assumptions.

Lemma 6.7
.

For
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
,

E_{K}=\int\left(|\nabla^{K}Q|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}U|^{2}\right)\phi^{K}{\rm d}y\leq\frac{E}{2}.

Proof.

For the sake of simplicity in the proof, we introduce a family of multi-indices
\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{i}

(i=0,1,\cdots\!,\ell)
for some integer
\ell\in[1,K]
and
\beta=(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\cdots,\beta_{K})
with
|\beta|=K
, which satisfy

\begin{split}&|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|+|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}|+\cdots+|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{\ell}|=|\beta|=K.\end{split}

Here
|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{i}|
, for
i=0,1,\cdots\!,\ell
, and
|\beta|
denote the lengths of the multi-indices
\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{i}
and
\beta
, respectively, as defined in §
2.1.1
.

E_{K}
can be rewritten as follows

E_{K}=\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\left(|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\right)\phi^{K}\text{d}y,

(6.40)

where
\partial_{\beta}U
is the vector
(\partial_{\beta}U_{1},\partial_{\beta}U_{2},\partial_{\beta}U_{3})^{\top}
.

Applying
\partial_{\beta}
to (
2.12
) and using the fact that

\partial_{\beta}(y\cdot\nabla f)=K\partial_{\beta}f+y\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}f,

we have

\displaystyle(\partial_{\tau}+\Lambda-1+K)\partial_{\beta}Q+y\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}Q+\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla Q)+\alpha\partial_{\beta}(Q\,\mathrm{div}U)=0,

(6.41)

\displaystyle(\partial_{\tau}+\Lambda-1+K)\partial_{\beta}U+y\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}U+\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla U)+\alpha\partial_{\beta}(Q\nabla Q)=\partial_{\beta}\mathcal{F}_{\rm dis}.

Multiplying each equation of (
6.41
) by
\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}Q
and
\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}U
respectively, summing over all
|\beta|=K
, and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}+\Lambda-1+K\big)E_{K}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}y\cdot\big(\partial_{\beta}Q\nabla\partial_{\beta}Q+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\nabla\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\big)\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\big(\partial_{\beta}Q\,\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla Q)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla U_{i})\big)\text{d}y

(6.42)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\alpha\phi^{K}\big(\partial_{\beta}Q\,\partial_{\beta}(Q\,\mathrm{div}\,U)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,\partial_{\beta}(Q\partial_{y_{i}}Q)\big)\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{|\beta|=K}C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}U\,\partial_{\beta}\big(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}L(U)+\frac{\delta}{\alpha}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\text{d}y

\displaystyle=-I_{1}-I_{2}-I_{3}+C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}I_{4}.

We divide the rest of the proof into four steps.

1
.
Estimate of
I_{1}
.
By integrating by parts, it follows from (
4.21
) that

\displaystyle-I_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}y\cdot\big(\nabla|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\nabla|\partial_{\beta}U_{i}|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

(6.43)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\frac{\mathrm{div}(\phi^{K}y)}{\phi^{K}}\big(|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}E+\frac{K}{2}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\frac{y\cdot\nabla\phi}{\phi}\big(|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y.

2
.
Estimate of
I_{2}
and
I_{3}
.
To reindex the summation, we denote by
\tilde{\beta}
a multi-index with
|\tilde{\beta}|=K-1
. Moreover, in preparation for applying the repulsivity properties (
B.6
)–(
B.7
)
of profile
(\overline{Q},\overline{U})
, we define
\nabla_{\theta}
as the angular gradient in spherical coordinates by

\frac{1}{r}\nabla_{\theta}:=\nabla-\frac{y}{r}\,\partial_{r},

(6.44)

where
r=|y|
and
\partial_{r}:=\frac{y}{r}\cdot\nabla
denotes
the radial derivative.
Using (
4.21
) and (
6.23
) yields

\displaystyle-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla U_{i})\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle=-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla U_{i})-U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}U_{i}-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{k=1}^{3}L_{k\beta_{j}}\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\Big)\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Big(U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}U_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{k=1}^{3}L_{k\beta_{j}}\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\Big)\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{|\beta|=K}\big(E^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{4K}}+C\|\partial_{\beta}U\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|\partial_{\beta}U\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{3}U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{|\beta^{(j)}|=K-1}\sum_{\beta_{j}=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\int\phi^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\bigg(L_{k\beta_{j}}\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\,\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\bigg)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3^{K}}{2}E^{1-\frac{1}{2K}}+C\sum_{|\beta|=K}\|\partial_{\beta}U\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\frac{\mathrm{div}(\phi^{K}U)}{2\phi^{K}}|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{|\beta^{(j)}|=K-1}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int\phi^{K}\big(\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}})-\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|}\big)\sum_{\beta_{j},k=1}^{3}\frac{y_{k}y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|^{2}}\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\,\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{|\beta^{(j)}|=K-1}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int\phi^{K}\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|}\sum_{k=1}^{3}|\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}|^{2}\,\text{d}y

(6.45)

\displaystyle\leq CE+\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\frac{K\nabla\phi\cdot U+\phi\,\mathrm{div}\,U}{2\phi}|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-K\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|=K-1}\int\phi^{K}\big(\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}})-\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|}\big)\sum_{i=1}^{3}|\partial_{r}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}U_{i}|^{2}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-K\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|=K-1}\sum_{i,k=1}^{3}\int\phi^{K}\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|}|\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}U_{i}|^{2}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq CE+\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\big(\frac{K\nabla\phi\cdot\widetilde{U}}{2\phi}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{div}\,U\big)|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+K\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|=K-1}\int\Big(\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot\widehat{X}\overline{U}}{2\phi}|\nabla\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}U|^{2}-\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}})|\partial_{r}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}U|^{2}-\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|^{3}}|\nabla_{\theta}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}U|^{2}\Big)\,\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq CE+K\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|=K-1}\int\Big(\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot\widehat{X}\overline{U}}{2\phi}|\nabla\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}U|^{2}-\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}})|\partial_{r}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}U|^{2}-\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|^{3}}|\nabla_{\theta}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}U|^{2}\Big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y,

where the second inequality (
i.e.
, the 6th line of (
6.45
)) follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, integration by parts, and rearranging the indices; in the third inequality (
i.e.
, the 9th line of (
6.45
)), we have used the identity:

\sum_{k,\beta_{j}=1}^{3}\frac{y_{k}y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|^{2}}\partial_{y_{k}}f\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}f=|\partial_{r}f|^{2};

and, in the last inequality (
i.e.
, the line 14 of (
6.45
)), we have employed the bounds:

\|\mathrm{div}\,U\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C,\qquad\big\|\frac{\nabla\phi}{2\phi}\widetilde{U}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\nu_{0}

with
\nu_{0}\ll K^{-1}
.

By an analogous argument, we obtain from (
4.21
) and (
6.24
) that

\displaystyle-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}Q\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla Q)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle=-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}Q\Big(\partial_{\beta}(U\cdot\nabla Q)-U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}Q-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{k=1}^{3}L_{k\beta_{j}}\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}Q\Big)\,\text{d}y

(6.46)

\displaystyle\ \quad-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}Q\Big(U\cdot\nabla\partial_{\beta}Q+\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{k=1}^{3}L_{k\beta_{j}}\partial_{y_{k}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}Q\Big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq CE+K\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|=K-1}\int\Big(\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot\widehat{X}\overline{U}}{2\phi}|\nabla\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}Q|^{2}-\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}})|\partial_{r}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}Q|^{2}-\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|^{3}}|\nabla_{\theta}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}Q|^{2}\Big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y.

Adding (
6.45
)–(
6.46
) yields

\displaystyle-I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq CE+K\int\Big(\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot\widehat{X}\overline{U}}{2\phi}-\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}})\Big)\big(|\partial_{r}\nabla^{K-1}U|^{2}+|\partial_{r}\nabla^{K-1}Q|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y

(6.47)

\displaystyle+K\int\Big(\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot\widehat{X}\overline{U}}{2\phi|y|^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|^{3}}\Big)\big(|\nabla_{\theta}\nabla^{K-1}U|^{2}+|\nabla_{\theta}\nabla^{K-1}Q|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y.

Then we start estimating
I_{3}
. It follows from (
4.21
) and (
6.25
) that

\displaystyle-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\partial_{\beta}(Q\partial_{y_{i}}Q)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle=-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\Big(\partial_{\beta}(Q\partial_{y_{i}}Q)-Q\partial_{\beta}\partial_{y_{i}}Q-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\big(\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\frac{y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|}\big)\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}Q\Big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\Big(Q\partial_{\beta}\partial_{y_{i}}Q+\sum_{j=1}^{K}\big(\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\frac{y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|}\big)\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}Q\Big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{|\beta|=K}\big(E^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{4K}}+C\|\partial_{\beta}Q\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}\big)\|\partial_{\beta}U\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}Q\nabla\partial_{\beta}Q\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{|\beta^{(j)}|=K-1}\sum_{\beta_{j}=1}^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int\phi^{K}\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\frac{y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|}\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}Q\,\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\,\text{d}y

(6.48)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3^{K}}{2}E^{1-\frac{1}{2K}}+C\sum_{|\beta|=K}\big(\|\partial_{\beta}Q\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{\beta}U\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}Q\nabla\partial_{\beta}Q\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{|\beta^{(j)}|=K-1}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int\phi^{K}\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\sum_{\beta_{j}=1}^{3}\frac{y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}U_{i}\,\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}Q\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq CE-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}Q\nabla\partial_{\beta}Q\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{K}{2}\sum_{|\tilde{\beta}|=K-1}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int\phi^{K}|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|\big(|\nabla\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}U_{i}|^{2}+|\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{\tilde{\beta}}Q|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle=CE-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}Q\nabla\partial_{\beta}Q\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\text{d}y+\frac{K}{2}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|\big(|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y.

By an analogous argument, it follows from (
4.21
) and (
6.26
) that

\displaystyle-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\partial_{\beta}(Q\partial_{y_{i}}U_{i})\partial_{\beta}Q\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle=-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\Big(\partial_{\beta}(Q\,\mathrm{div}\,U)-Q\partial_{\beta}\,\mathrm{div}\,U-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\big(\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\frac{y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|}\big)\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}(\mathrm{div}U)\Big)\partial_{\beta}Q\,\text{d}y

(6.49)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\Big(Q\partial_{\beta}\,\mathrm{div}\,U+\sum_{j=1}^{K}\big(\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})\frac{y_{\beta_{j}}}{|y|}\big)\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}(\mathrm{div}U)\Big)\partial_{\beta}Q\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq CE-\sum_{|\beta|=K}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\int\phi^{K}Q\partial_{\beta}Q\cdot\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{\beta}U_{i}\,\text{d}y+\frac{K}{2}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|\big(|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y.

Combining (
6.48
)–(
6.49
) and integrating by parts, along with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, give

\displaystyle-I_{3}

\displaystyle\leq CE+\alpha\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\frac{\nabla(\phi^{K}Q)}{\phi^{K}}\cdot\big(\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}Q\partial_{\beta}U\big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha K\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|\big(|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq CE+\alpha\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\Big(\frac{K\nabla\phi\widetilde{Q}}{\phi}+\frac{K\nabla\phi\widehat{X}\overline{Q}}{\phi}+\nabla Q\Big)\cdot\big(\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}Q\partial_{\beta}U\big)\,\text{d}y

(6.50)

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha K\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|\big(|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq CE+K\alpha\sum_{|\beta|=K}\int\phi^{K}\Big(\frac{|\nabla\phi|\widehat{X}\overline{Q}}{2\phi}+|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|\Big)\big(|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y,

where we have used the following facts in the last inequality (
i.e.
, the line 5th of (
6.1.2
)):

\|\nabla Q\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C,\qquad\|K\frac{\nabla\phi}{2\phi}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq CK\nu_{0}\leq 1.

3
.
Estimate of
I_{4}
.
By the expression of
I_{4}
, after commuting
\partial_{\beta}
with
Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}
and
Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}
and then integrating by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{4,\beta}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int\phi^{K}\big[\partial_{\beta},Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big]L(U)\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\text{d}y}_{\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta}}-\underbrace{\int\big(\nabla(\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}})\cdot\partial_{\beta}\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\text{d}y}_{\mathcal{D}_{2,\beta}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\int\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big(a_{1}|\nabla\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\mathrm{div}\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y}_{\mathcal{D}_{3,\beta}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\int\phi^{K}\big[\partial_{\beta},Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\big]\big(\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\text{d}y}_{\mathcal{D}_{4,\beta}}

(6.51)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\int\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\big(\partial_{\beta}(\nabla Q)\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\mathrm{d}y}_{\mathcal{D}_{5,\beta}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\int\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\big(\partial_{\beta}(\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U))-\partial_{\beta}(\nabla Q)\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\mathrm{d}y}_{\mathcal{D}_{6,\beta}}.

Since
\mathcal{D}_{3,\beta}>0
, this is a dissipative term and already carries the favorable sign. We now estimate the remaining terms, starting with
\mathcal{D}_{2,\beta}
. Applying (
4.21
) and the Hölder inequality yields

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{2,\beta}|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\int\Big(\big(K\phi^{K-1}\nabla\phi\,Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}+\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\phi^{K}\nabla Q\big)\cdot\partial_{\beta}\mathbb{D}(U)\Big)\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\text{d}y\bigg|

(6.52)

\displaystyle\leq C\,\mathcal{D}_{3,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}K\Big(\int\phi^{K-2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\,\text{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+C\,\mathcal{D}_{3,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}\Big(\int\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\frac{|\nabla Q|^{2}}{Q^{2}}|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\,\text{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\mathcal{D}_{3,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}E^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(K\Big\|\frac{|\nabla\phi|^{2}}{\phi^{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}+\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}\Big\|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\frac{|\nabla Q|^{2}}{Q^{2}}\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\bigg).

It follows from Lemmas
5.2
–
5.3
that

\displaystyle\frac{|\nabla\phi|^{2}}{\phi^{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-2+\frac{(1-\delta)}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)}=C(\nu_{0})\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-\Lambda+\delta_{\rm{dis}}}\leq C(\nu_{0}),

\displaystyle\frac{|\nabla Q|^{2}}{Q^{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\big\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\big\rangle^{-2+\frac{(1-\delta)}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)}\leq C(\nu_{0}),

where we have used the fact that

-\Lambda+\delta_{\rm{dis}}<0.

Since
\nu_{0}
is sufficiently small and depends only on
E
and
K
, we obtain

|\mathcal{D}_{2,\beta}|\leq C(\nu_{0})\mathcal{D}_{3,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(6.53)

For
\mathcal{D}_{5,\beta}
, it follows from integration by parts that

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{5,\beta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\int\partial_{\beta}Q\big(\nabla(\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1})\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\text{d}y

(6.54)

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\int\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\partial_{\beta}Q\,\mathrm{div}(\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\mathbb{D}(U))\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle=\mathcal{D}_{5,\beta,1}+\mathcal{D}_{5,\beta,2}.

We first estimate
\mathcal{D}_{5,\beta,1}
. It follows from (
4.21
) and Lemmas
5.2
–
5.3
that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{5,\beta,1}|

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\bigg|\int\partial_{\beta}Q\Big(\big(K\phi^{K-1}\nabla\phi\,Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}+(\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1)\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-2}\nabla Q\big)\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\Big)\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\text{d}y\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq E^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(K\Big(\int\phi^{K-2}|\nabla\phi|^{2}Q^{\frac{2(\delta-1)}{\alpha}}\frac{|\mathbb{D}(U)|^{2}}{Q^{2}}|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}\,\text{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg.

\displaystyle\quad\bigg.+\big(\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}+1\big)\Big(\int\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{2(\delta-1)}{\alpha}}\frac{|\nabla Q|^{2}}{Q^{2}}\frac{|\mathbb{D}(U)|^{2}}{Q^{2}}|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}\,\text{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)

(6.55)

\displaystyle\leq E\bigg(K\Big\|\frac{|\nabla\phi|^{2}}{\phi^{2}}\frac{|\mathbb{D}(U)|^{2}}{Q^{2}}Q^{\frac{2(\delta-1)}{\alpha}}\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}+\big(\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}+1\big)\Big\|\frac{|\nabla Q|^{2}}{Q^{2}}\frac{|\mathbb{D}(U)|^{2}}{Q^{2}}Q^{\frac{2(\delta-1)}{\alpha}}\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0}),

where we used the facts that

\max\big\{|\frac{\nabla Q}{Q}|,\ |\frac{\nabla U}{U}|,\ |\frac{\nabla\phi}{\phi}|\big\}\leq C(\nu_{0})\langle\frac{y}{R_{0}}\rangle^{-1},\qquad-\Lambda+\delta_{\rm{dis}}<0,

and
\nu_{0}
is sufficiently small and depends on
E
and
K
.

Next, for
\mathcal{D}_{5,\beta,2}
, we obtain from (
4.21
), Lemmas
5.2
–
5.4
, and
\nu_{0}\ll\frac{1}{E}\ll\frac{1}{K}
that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{5,\beta,2}|

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\Big|\int\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\partial_{\beta}Q\,\mathrm{div}\,\big(\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\,\text{d}y\Big|

\displaystyle\leq CE^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{2(\delta-1)}{\alpha}}\frac{|\nabla^{2}U|^{2}}{Q^{2}}|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\bigg.+C\,\mathcal{D}_{3,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\frac{|\nabla U|^{2}}{Q^{2}}|\partial_{\beta}Q|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(6.56)

\displaystyle\leq CE\Big\|\frac{|\nabla^{2}U|^{2}}{Q^{2}}Q^{\frac{2(\delta-1)}{\alpha}}\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}+C\,\mathcal{D}_{3,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}E^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|\frac{|\nabla U|^{2}}{Q^{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\big(1+\mathcal{D}_{3,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big).

Substituting (
6.55
)–(
6.56
) into (
6.54
) leads to

\mathcal{D}_{5,\beta}\leq C(\nu_{0})\big(1+\mathcal{D}_{3,\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big).

(6.57)

We now turn to the estimate of
\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta}
in (
6.1.2
). Note that

\begin{split}&\Big|\big[\partial_{\beta},Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big]f-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}f\Big|\\
&\leq C(K)3^{K}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{j}|=K\\
0\leq|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|\leq K-2\end{subarray}}\Big|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}}f\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q}{Q}\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{2}}Q}{Q}\cdots\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{\ell}}Q}{Q}\Big|,\end{split}

where
|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{j}|\geq 1
for all
1\leq j\leq\ell
.

Based on the above inequality and (
4.21
), we can decompose
\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{K}\int|\phi^{K}\partial_{\beta}U\cdot\partial_{\beta^{(j)}}L(U)Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\partial_{y_{\beta_{j}}}Q|\,\text{d}y

(6.58)

\displaystyle+C(K)3^{K}E^{\frac{1}{2}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{j}|=K\\
0\leq|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|\leq K-2\end{subarray}}\Big\|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}}L(U)\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q}{Q}\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{2}}Q}{Q}\cdots\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{\ell}}Q}{Q}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta,1}+\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta,2}.

For later use, we define

\mathcal{D}_{3}=\sum_{|\beta|=K}\mathcal{D}_{3,\beta}=\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\phi^{K}\big(a_{1}|\nabla^{K+1}U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\nabla^{K}\mathrm{div}U|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y.

By (
4.21
), Lemmas
5.2
–
5.3
, and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we first estimate
\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta,1}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta,1}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\int\phi^{K}|\partial_{\beta}U|\,|\nabla^{K+1}U|\frac{|\nabla Q|}{Q}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\,\text{d}y

(6.59)

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\mathcal{D}_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int\phi^{K}|\partial_{\beta}U|^{2}\frac{|\nabla Q|^{2}}{Q^{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\,\text{d}y\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(K)\mathcal{D}_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}E^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|\frac{|\nabla Q|^{2}}{Q^{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(\nu_{0})\mathcal{D}_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We now proceed to estimate
\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta,2}
by splitting the analysis into four cases.

Generic case.
0\leq|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|\leq K-4
and
1\leq|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{i}|\leq K-2
for all
i\geq 1
.
In this case, each term involves less than
(K-2)
-th order derivatives. By Lemmas
5.2
–
5.4
, we derive

\displaystyle\Big\|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}}L(U)\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q}{Q}\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{2}}Q}{Q}\cdots\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{\ell}}Q}{Q}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\big\|\phi^{-1}\langle y\rangle^{(\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}-1)(\Lambda-1)}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{(K+2)\Lambda-(\ell+1)\Lambda-(\ell+1)K+\frac{5}{2}(\ell+1)+(1-\eta)(K(\ell+1)-\frac{5}{2}K-5)}{K-5/2}}\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=C(\nu_{0})\big\|\phi^{-1}\langle y\rangle^{(\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}-1)(\Lambda-1)}\langle y\rangle^{\frac{\Lambda(K+1-\ell)-\frac{5}{2}(1-\eta)(K+2)-K\eta(\ell+1)+\frac{5}{2}(\ell+1)}{K-5/2}}\big\|_{L^{2}}.

If
\ell\geq 2
, since
K
is sufficiently large with respect to
\eta
, we have

(\frac{3}{2}-\ell)K\eta<-100\ell,

which implies

\Lambda(K+1-\ell)-\frac{5}{2}(1-\eta)(K+2)-K\eta(\ell+1)+\frac{5}{2}(\ell+1)\leq(\Lambda-\frac{5}{2})K.

(6.60)

If
\ell=1
, a similar computation yields

\Lambda(K+1-l)-\frac{5}{2}(1-\eta)(K+2)-K\eta(l+1)+\frac{5}{2}(l+1)\leq(\Lambda-\frac{5}{2}+\eta)K.

(6.61)

Using
\eta
is sufficiently small,
-1+\delta_{\rm{dis}}<0
, and
\Lambda>1
, we deduce

\displaystyle\big\|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}}L(U)\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q}{Q}\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{2}}Q}{Q}\cdots\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{\ell}}Q}{Q}\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\big\|\langle y\rangle^{-2(1-\eta)+(\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}-1)(\Lambda-1)+\Lambda-\frac{5}{2}+\eta}\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=C(\nu_{0})\big\|\langle y\rangle^{-\Lambda-\frac{3}{2}+3\eta+\delta_{\rm{dis}}}\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=C(\nu_{0})\Big(\int_{0}^{\infty}\langle r\rangle^{-2\Lambda-3+6\eta+2\delta_{\rm{dis}}}r^{2}\text{d}r\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C(\nu_{0}).

Case where some
|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{i}|\in\{K-1,K\}
for
i\geq 1
.
Without loss of generality, we assume
i=1
. If
|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}|=K
, then, by (
4.21
) and Lemmas
5.2
–
5.4
, we have

\Big\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q\frac{L(U)}{Q}\Big\|_{L^{2}}\leq CE^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\frac{|\nabla^{2}U|}{Q}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(\nu_{0}).

If
|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}|=K-1
, we divide the corresponding estimates into two subcases. According to Lemmas
5.2
–
5.4
, we see that, for fixed
j\in\{1,2,3\}
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q\frac{\partial_{y_{j}}Q\,L(U)}{Q^{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon})\|\langle y\rangle^{K(1-\eta)\frac{K-2}{K-1}-\bar{\varepsilon}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q\|_{L^{2+\frac{2}{K-2}}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big\|\frac{|\nabla Q|\langle y\rangle}{Q}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big\|\frac{\langle y\rangle^{K(1-\eta)\frac{1}{K-1}+\bar{\varepsilon}-1}|\nabla^{2}U|}{Q}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\Big\|_{L^{2(K-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon})\big\|\langle y\rangle^{-2(1-\eta)+\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)-1+\frac{K}{K-1}(1-\eta)+\bar{\varepsilon}}\big\|_{L^{2(K-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon})\big\|\langle y\rangle^{-\Lambda+2\eta+\bar{\varepsilon}+\frac{K}{K-1}(1-\eta)}\big\|_{L^{2(K-1)}}\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon}),

\displaystyle\Big\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q\frac{\partial_{y_{j}}L(U)}{Q}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon})\|\langle y\rangle^{K(1-\eta)\frac{K-2}{K-1}-\bar{\varepsilon}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}Q\|_{L^{2+\frac{2}{K-2}}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Big\|\frac{\langle y\rangle^{K(1-\eta)\frac{1}{K-1}+\bar{\varepsilon}}|\nabla^{3}U|}{Q}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\Big\|_{L^{2(K-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon})\big\|\langle y\rangle^{-3(1-\eta)+\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)+\frac{K}{K-1}(1-\eta)+\bar{\varepsilon}}\big\|_{L^{2(K-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon})\big\|\langle y\rangle^{-\Lambda+3\eta+\bar{\varepsilon}+\frac{K}{K-1}(1-\eta)}\big\|_{L^{2(K-1)}}\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon}).

Case where
|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|=K-2
.

We obtain from (
4.21
) and Lemmas
5.2
–
5.4
that

\displaystyle\Big\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}}L(U)\nabla^{2}(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}})\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\nabla^{K}U\|_{L^{2}}\Big(\Big\|\frac{\nabla^{2}Q}{Q}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}+\Big\|\frac{|\nabla Q|^{2}}{Q^{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq E^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\big\|\langle y\rangle^{-2(1-\eta)+\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}+\big\|\langle y\rangle^{-2+\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\leq C(\nu_{0}).

Case where
|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|=K-3
.

It follows from Lemmas
5.2
–
5.4
that

\displaystyle\big\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}}L(U)\nabla^{3}(Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}})\big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon})\big\|\langle y\rangle^{K(1-\eta)\frac{K-2}{K-1}-\bar{\varepsilon}}\nabla^{K-1}U\big\|_{L^{1+\frac{2}{K-2}}}\,\big\|\langle y\rangle^{(1-\eta)\frac{K}{K-1}+\bar{\varepsilon}+\frac{1-\delta}{\alpha}(\Lambda-1)-3(1-\eta)}\big\|_{L^{2(K-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon})\big\|\langle y\rangle^{-\Lambda+3\eta+\bar{\varepsilon}+(1-\eta)\frac{K}{K-1}}\big\|_{L^{2(K-1)}}\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon}).

Collecting the above estimates and choosing
\bar{\varepsilon}
sufficiently small (depending on
\eta
,
\Lambda
, and
K
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta,2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0},\bar{\varepsilon})\leq C(\nu_{0}).

(6.62)

Repeating the procedure used in the estimate of
\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta,2}
, we deduce

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{4,\beta}\leq C(\nu_{0}).

(6.63)

For
D_{6,\beta}
, it follows from (
4.21
) and Lemmas
5.2
–
5.3
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{6,\beta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\alpha}\int\phi^{K}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}-1}\big(\partial_{\beta}(\nabla Q\cdot\mathbb{D}(U))-\partial_{\beta}(\nabla Q)\cdot\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\cdot\partial_{\beta}U\,\mathrm{d}y

(6.64)

\displaystyle\leq CE^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{D}_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|\frac{|\nabla Q|^{2}}{Q^{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\Big\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}}+C(K)E^{\frac{1}{2}}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|+|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}|=K\\
1\leq|\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}|\leq K-1\end{subarray}}\Big\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{0}}\mathbb{D}(U)\frac{\partial_{\bm{\bar{\beta}}^{1}}\nabla Q}{Q}\Big\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})\mathcal{D}_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathcal{D}_{6,\beta,2}\leq C(\nu_{0})\big(1+\mathcal{D}_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big).

The estimate of
\mathcal{D}_{6,\beta,2}
follows from the same argument as
\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta,2}
and is therefore omitted.

Consequently, it follows from (
6.53
)–(
6.64
) that

\displaystyle C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}I_{4}+C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\mathcal{D}_{3}

\displaystyle=C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\sum_{|\beta|=K}\big(\mathcal{D}_{1,\beta}-\mathcal{D}_{2,\beta}+\mathcal{D}_{4,\beta}+\mathcal{D}_{5,\beta}+\mathcal{D}_{6,\beta}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\big(\mathcal{D}_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\big),

where
C(\nu_{0})
is sufficiently large and depends on
\nu_{0}
. Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives

C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}I_{4}+C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\mathcal{D}_{3}\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}+e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\Big(\mathcal{D}_{3}\frac{C_{\rm{dis}}}{2}+\frac{2C(\nu_{0})^{2}}{C_{\rm{dis}}}\Big).

Consequently,

C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}I_{4}+\frac{1}{2}C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\mathcal{D}_{3}\leq e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\Big(C(\nu_{0})+\frac{2C(\nu_{0})^{2}}{C_{\rm{dis}}}\Big)\leq C(\nu_{0})e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}.

Since
\tau_{0}
is sufficiently large, depending on
\nu_{0}
, we conclude

C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}I_{4}+\frac{1}{2}C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\mathcal{D}_{3}\leq e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

(6.65)

4
.
Energy estimate
.
It follows from the estimates for
I_{1},I_{2},I_{3},C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}I_{4}
obtained above, (
6.1.2
), and (
6.44
) that

\displaystyle\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}+\Lambda-1+K\big)E_{K}+\frac{1}{2}C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\phi^{K}\big(a_{1}|\nabla^{K+1}U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\nabla^{K}\mathrm{div}U|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle=-I_{1}-I_{2}-I_{3}+C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}I_{4}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\phi^{K}\big(a_{1}|\nabla^{K+1}U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\nabla^{K}\mathrm{div}U|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq CE+\frac{K}{2}\int\frac{y\cdot\nabla\phi}{\phi}\big(|\nabla^{K}Q|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y+e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}(\tau-\tau_{0})}

\displaystyle\quad+K\int\Big(\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot\widehat{X}\overline{U}}{2\phi}-\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}})\Big)\big(|\partial_{r}\nabla^{K-1}Q|^{2}+|\partial_{r}\nabla^{K-1}U|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+K\int\Big(\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot\widehat{X}\overline{U}}{2\phi|y|^{2}}-\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|^{3}}\Big)\big(|\nabla_{\theta}\nabla^{K-1}U|^{2}+|\nabla_{\theta}\nabla^{K-1}Q|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+K\alpha\int\phi^{K}\Big(\frac{|\nabla\phi|\widehat{X}\overline{Q}}{\phi}+|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|\Big)\big(|\nabla^{K}U|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}Q|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

(6.66)

\displaystyle\leq CE+e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}(\tau-\tau_{0})}

\displaystyle\quad+K\int\big(-\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}})+\alpha|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|\big)\big(|\partial_{r}\nabla^{K-1}U|^{2}+|\partial_{r}\nabla^{K-1}Q|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+K\int\Big(-\frac{\widehat{X}\overline{\mathcal{U}}}{|y|^{3}}+\frac{\alpha|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|}{|y|^{2}}\Big)\big(|\nabla_{\theta}\nabla^{K-1}U|^{2}+|\nabla_{\theta}\nabla^{K-1}Q|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+K\int\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot(y+\widehat{X}\overline{U})+|\nabla\phi|\widehat{X}\overline{Q}}{2\phi}\big(|\nabla^{K}U|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}Q|^{2}\big)\phi^{K}\,\text{d}y.

In
B(0,R_{0})
, the weight function satisfies
\phi=1
. Moreover, we know that

|\partial_{r}\widehat{X}|\leq Ce^{-\tau_{0}}
for
r=|y|
. Therefore, by (
4.1
)–(
4.2
) and the standard repulsivity properties (
B.6
)–(
B.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle-\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\mathcal{\overline{U}})+\alpha|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|+\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot(y+\widehat{X}\overline{U})+|\nabla\phi|\widehat{X}\overline{Q}}{2\phi}\leq 1-\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{2},

\displaystyle-\frac{\widehat{X}\mathcal{\overline{U}}}{r}+\alpha|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|+\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot(y+\widehat{X}\overline{U})+|\nabla\phi|\widehat{X}\overline{Q}}{2\phi}\leq 1-\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{2},

where
r=|y|
.
In the exterior region
B^{c}(0,R_{0})
, it follows from (
2.7
), (
4.1
), (
4.3
), and (
5.5
) that

-\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\mathcal{\overline{U}})+\alpha|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|+\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot(y+\overline{U})+|\nabla\phi|\overline{Q}}{2\phi}\leq\frac{\eta}{4}+\frac{r\partial_{r}\phi}{2\phi}\leq\frac{\eta}{4}+1-\eta<1-\frac{\eta}{2},

and

-\frac{\widehat{X}\mathcal{\overline{U}}}{r}+\alpha|\partial_{r}(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|+\frac{\nabla\phi\cdot(y+\overline{U})+|\nabla\phi|\overline{Q}}{2\phi}\leq\frac{\eta}{4}+\frac{r\partial_{r}\phi}{2\phi}\leq\frac{\eta}{4}+1-\eta<1-\frac{\eta}{2},

where
r=|y|
.
Combining the above estimates, we derive

\displaystyle\big(\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}+\Lambda-1+K\big)E_{K}+\frac{1}{2}C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\phi^{K}\big(a_{1}|\nabla^{K+1}U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\nabla^{K}\mathrm{div}U|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

\displaystyle\leq CE+K\Big(1-\frac{\min\{\tilde{\eta},\eta\}}{2}\Big)E_{K}+e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Equivalently,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}E_{K}+C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\phi^{K}\big(a_{1}|\nabla^{K+1}U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\nabla^{K}\mathrm{div}U|^{2}\big)\,\text{d}y

(6.67)

\displaystyle\leq-K\min\{\tilde{\eta},\eta\}E_{K}+CE.

Since
K
is sufficiently large and depends on
(\eta,\tilde{\eta})
, applying the Grönwall inequality yields

\displaystyle E_{K}(\tau)+C\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tilde{\tau}-K\min\{\tilde{\eta},\eta\}(\tau-\tilde{\tau})}\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{2\alpha}}\nabla^{K+1}U(\tilde{\tau},\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\text{d}\tilde{\tau}

(6.68)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-K\min\{\tilde{\eta},\eta\}(\tau-\tau_{0})}E_{K}(\tau_{0})+\frac{CE}{K\min\{\tilde{\eta},\eta\}}\big(1-e^{-K\min\{\tilde{\eta},\eta\}(\tau-\tau_{0})}\big)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-K\min\{\tilde{\eta},\eta\}(\tau-\tau_{0})}E_{K}(\tau_{0})+\frac{E}{2}\big(1-e^{-K\min\{\tilde{\eta},\eta\}(\tau-\tau_{0})}\big).

If
E_{K}(\tau_{0})\leq E/2
, then
E_{K}(\tau)\leq E/2
for all
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
.

This completes the proof of Lemma
6.7
.
∎

6.1.3.
Control of the unstable modes of
(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})
by the selection of initial data

Since the truncated linear operator
\mathcal{L}
generates a semigroup on space
X
of functions supported in
B(0,3C_{0})
, we consider the Cauchy problem (
4.14
) with initial data

(\overset{\approx}{Q}_{0},\overset{\approx}{U}_{0})
.
Recalling the invariant decomposition

X=V_{\mathrm{sta}}\oplus V_{\mathrm{uns}},

the initial data for (
4.14
) admit a decomposition into stable and unstable components. The stable component has already been chosen in §
4.2
so that its evolution is controlled by the estimates established above.

We now turn to the selection of appropriate unstable components in order to control the solution to (
4.14
).
Recall that
\{(\varphi_{i,\zeta},\varphi_{i,u})\}_{i=1}^{N}
forms a normalized basis of
V_{\rm{uns}}
. For any
(\overset{\approx}{Q}(\cdot,\tau),\overset{\approx}{U}(\cdot,\tau))
, we denote

k(\tau)=P_{\rm{uns}}(\overset{\approx}{Q}(\cdot,\tau),\overset{\approx}{U}(\cdot,\tau))=\sum_{i=1}^{N}k_{i}(\tau)(\varphi_{i,\zeta},\varphi_{i,u}).

By the initial data condition (
4.15
), together with (
\chi_{2}\widetilde{U}_{0}^{*},\chi_{2}\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*})\in V_{\rm{sta}}
, we have

k_{i}(\tau_{0})=\hat{k}_{i}.

From Lemma
C.3
, there exists a metric
\Upsilon
such that

\langle\mathcal{L}k(\tau),k(\tau)\rangle_{\Upsilon}\geq-\frac{3\nu_{g}}{5}\langle k(\tau),k(\tau)\rangle_{\Upsilon}.

Since the unstable space
V_{\rm{uns}}
only has finite dimension and
X\subset H^{m}(B(0,3C_{0}))
,
all the norms on
V_{\rm{uns}}
are equivalent.
In particular, there exists a constant
C(m)>0
such that

\|k(\tau)\|_{X}\leq C(m)\|k(\tau)\|_{\Upsilon}\leq C(m)\|k(\tau)\|_{X},

(6.69)

where we have denoted the norm
\|k(\tau)\|_{X}:=\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}k_{i}(\tau)\varphi_{i}\|_{X}
, and
the dependence on
m
arises from the definition of space
X
.

We now restrict the initial coefficients
\hat{k}_{i}
such that

\|\hat{k}\|_{\Upsilon}\leq\nu_{1}^{\frac{11}{10}}.

By (
6.69
), this yields

\|\hat{k}\|_{X}\leq C(m)\nu_{1}^{\frac{11}{10}}.

Since
\varphi_{i}
are compactly supported,
\|\varphi_{i}\|_{X}=1
, and
\nu_{1}
is sufficiently small (depending on
m
and
E
), the initial conditions (
4.12
)–(
4.13
) are automatically satisfied for any such choice of
\hat{k}_{i}
as long as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\max\big\{\|\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*}\|_{L^{\infty}},\ \|\widetilde{U}_{0}^{*}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big\}\leq\nu_{1},\qquad\|\nabla^{K}\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*}\phi^{K/2}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{K}\widetilde{U}_{0}^{*}\phi^{K/2}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\frac{E}{4},\\
&\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*}+\widehat{X}\overline{Q}\geq\frac{\nu_{1}}{2},\qquad|\nabla(\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*}+\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|+|\nabla(\widetilde{U}_{0}^{*}+\widehat{X}\overline{U})|\leq C\langle y\rangle^{-\Lambda}.\end{split}

(6.70)

Let

\mathcal{R}(\tau)=\big\{k(\tau):\,\|k(\tau)\|_{X}\leq\nu_{1}^{\frac{21}{20}}e^{-\frac{4}{3}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\big\},

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\tau)=\big\{k(\tau):\,\|k(\tau)\|_{\Upsilon}\leq\nu_{1}^{\frac{11}{10}}e^{-\frac{4}{3}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\big\}.

By (
6.69
) and the choice of parameter (
4.1
), we obtain

\widetilde{R}(\tau)\Subset\mathcal{R}(\tau).

Throughout the argument, we choose the initial coefficients
\{\hat{k}_{i}\}=k(\tau_{0})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\tau_{0})
. As long as
k(\tau)
remains in
\mathcal{R}(\tau)
, the unstable modes are controlled.
Then we have the estimate for the forcing term:

\|\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}\|_{X}=\|(\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}_{\zeta},\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}_{u})\|_{X}\leq C\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

(6.71)

Next, we show the following outgoing property:

Lemma 6.8
.

Suppose that
k(\tau)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\tau)
at
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
, and

k(\tau_{*})\in\partial{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\tau_{*})}
at time
\tau_{*}
, that is,

\|k(\tau_{*})\|_{\Upsilon}=\nu_{1}^{\frac{11}{10}}e^{-\frac{4}{3}\varepsilon(\tau_{*}-\tau_{0})}.

Then
k(\tau)
does not belong to
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\tau)

for
\tau
close enough to
\tau_{*}
from above.

Proof.

Since
(\overset{\approx}{Q},\overset{\approx}{U})
satisfies the Cauchy problem (
4.14
)
and
V_{\rm{uns}}
is invariant with respect to the linear operator
\mathcal{L}
, the unstable component
k(\tau)
satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}k(\tau)=\mathcal{L}k(\tau)+P_{\rm{uns}}(\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}),\\
\displaystyle k(\tau_{0})=(\,\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{k}_{i}\varphi_{i,\zeta},\,\sum_{i=1}^{N}\hat{k}_{i}\varphi_{i,u}).\end{cases}

The forcing estimate (
6.71
) leads to

\displaystyle\|P_{\rm{uns}}(\chi_{2}\mathcal{F})\|_{\Upsilon}\leq C(m)\|P_{\rm{uns}}(\chi_{2}\mathcal{F})\|_{X}\leq C(m)\|\chi_{2}\mathcal{F}\|_{X}\leq C(m)\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}.

Consequently, we obtain from Lemma
C.3
that

\displaystyle\big\langle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}k(\tau),\,k(\tau)\big\rangle_{\Upsilon}\bigg|_{\tau=\tau_{*}}\geq-\frac{3\nu_{g}}{5}\langle k(\tau_{*}),k(\tau_{*})\rangle_{\Upsilon}-C(m)\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\varepsilon(\tau_{*}-\tau_{0})}\|k(\tau_{*})\|_{\Upsilon}.

Since
\|k(\tau_{*})\|_{\Upsilon}=\nu_{1}^{\frac{11}{10}}e^{-\frac{4}{3}\varepsilon(\tau_{*}-\tau_{0})}
,
it follows that

\displaystyle\big\langle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}k(\tau),\,k(\tau)\big\rangle_{\Upsilon}\bigg|_{\tau=\tau_{*}}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{3\nu_{g}}{5}\langle k(\tau_{*}),k(\tau_{*})\rangle_{\Upsilon}-C(m)\nu_{1}^{\frac{6}{5}-\frac{11}{10}}e^{-\varepsilon(\frac{3}{2}-\frac{4}{3})(\tau_{*}-\tau_{0})}\langle k(\tau_{*}),k(\tau_{*})\rangle_{\Upsilon}.

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{31}{50}\nu_{g}\langle k(\tau_{*}),k(\tau_{*})\rangle_{\Upsilon}.

When
\tau=\tau_{*}
,

\frac{31}{50}\nu_{g}<\frac{4}{3}\varepsilon=\frac{16}{25}\nu_{g}

yields

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\big(\langle k(\tau),\,k(\tau)\rangle_{\Upsilon}e^{\frac{8}{3}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}\big)\geq-\frac{31}{25}\nu_{g}\langle k(\tau),k(\tau)\rangle_{\Upsilon}e^{\frac{8}{3}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}+\frac{8}{3}\varepsilon\langle k(\tau),k(\tau)\rangle_{\Upsilon}e^{\frac{8}{3}\varepsilon(\tau-\tau_{0})}>0.

Therefore,
k(\tau)
exits
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\tau)
at
\tau=\tau_{*}
.

This completes the proof.

∎

We claim that coefficients
\{\hat{k}_{i}\}
can be selected so that
k(\tau)\in\mathcal{R}(\tau)
for all
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\infty)
. The argument is identical to that of
[
2
, Proposition 8.15]
, and thus we use that result directly.

Proposition 6.1
.

There exist specific initial conditions
\{\hat{k}_{i}\}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}(\tau_{0})
such that
k(\tau_{0})=\{\hat{k}_{i}\}
and
k(\tau)\in\mathcal{R}(\tau)
for all
\tau>\tau_{0}
.

Moreover, restriction
(\chi_{2}\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*},\chi_{2}\widetilde{U}_{0}^{*})\in V_{\mathrm{sta}}
is equivalent to

P_{\mathrm{uns}}(\widetilde{Q}_{0}^{*},\widetilde{U}_{0}^{*})=0,

which is a finite-dimensional closed restriction. Since projection

P_{\mathrm{uns}}
maps onto the finite-dimensional subspace

V_{\mathrm{uns}}
, the manifold of initial data that give rise to finite-time implosion has finite codimension.

The proof of Proposition
4.1
is now complete. By a standard continuity argument, the bootstrap assumptions are therefore preserved on the whole time interval of the existence. This enables us to establish the global-in-time existence of solution
(Q,U)
of the Cauchy problem (
2.12
)–(
2.16
).

6.2.
Global-In-Time Regularity

In this subsection, we establish the global-in-time regularity of the solution.
In §
6.2
, the initial data function
(\widetilde{Q}_{0},\widetilde{U}_{0})
under consideration
is constructed in (
4.10
)–(
4.13
),

1<\gamma<1+\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}
,
\Lambda\in(1,\Lambda^{*}(\gamma))
is introduced in Lemma
B.1
,
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
satisfies
\delta_{\rm dis}>0
,
and assumptions (
4.1
)–(
4.4
) always hold.
Based on the conclusions obtained in Proposition
4.1
,
we are now ready to establish the desired global-in-time regularities.

Lemma 6.9
.

The solution of the Cauchy problem (
2.12
)
–
(
2.16
) satisfies (
4.18
)
–
(
4.21
) and, for
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]
,

\|Q-Q^{*}\|^{2}_{H^{K}}+\|U\|^{2}_{H^{K}}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\|U(\tilde{\tau},\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{K+1}}\,{\rm d}\tilde{\tau}\leq C(\tau),

(6.72)

\|Q_{\tau}\|_{H^{K-1}}+\|U_{\tau}\|_{H^{K-2}}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\|U_{\tau}(\tilde{\tau},\cdot)\|_{H^{K-1}}\,{\rm d}\tilde{\tau}\leq C(\tau).

(6.73)

Proof.

Combining (
2.12
) with
Q^{*}=\Lambda e^{-(\Lambda-1)\tau}\bar{c}
, we derive

\displaystyle(\partial_{\tau}+\Lambda-1)(Q-Q^{*})=

\displaystyle-(y+U)\cdot\nabla(Q-Q^{*})-\alpha Q\,\mathrm{div}\,U,

(6.74)

\displaystyle(\partial_{\tau}+\Lambda-1)U=

\displaystyle-(y+U)\cdot\nabla U-\alpha Q\nabla Q+\mathcal{F}_{\rm{dis}}.

Multiplying each equation of (
6.74
) by
Q-Q^{*}
and
U
respectively, summing over all, and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{\rm d\tau}\int\big(|Q-Q^{*}|^{2}+|U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=-(\Lambda-1)\int\big(|Q-Q^{*}|^{2}+|U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y\,-\frac{1}{2}\int(y+U)\cdot\nabla\big(|Q-Q^{*}|^{2}+|U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y

(6.75)

\displaystyle\quad-\alpha\int\big(Q(Q-Q^{*})\,\mathrm{div}\,U+Q\nabla Q\cdot U\big)\,\mathrm{d}y+C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}}\int Q^{-\frac{1}{\delta}}\mathrm{div}\,\big(Q^{\frac{\delta}{\alpha}}\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\cdot U\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}+J_{4}.

For simplicity, we denote

\bar{E}(\tau)=\frac{1}{2}\int\big(|Q-Q^{*}|^{2}+|U|^{2}\big)(\tau,y)\,\mathrm{d}y,

where
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
.
Since
\Lambda>1
,
J_{1}
carries the negative sign. We now estimate the remaining terms.
Integrating by parts and using Lemma
5.3
, we have

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int(y+U)\cdot\nabla\big(|Q-Q^{*}|^{2}+|U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y

(6.76)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{div}(y+U)\big(|Q-Q^{*}|^{2}+|U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y\leq C\bar{E}(\tau).

For
J_{3}
, it follows from the Young inequality, Lemmas
5.2
and
6.3
,
the definition of
\widehat{X}
, and the properties of profile
(\overline{Q},\overline{U})
that

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle=-\alpha\int\big(Q(Q-Q^{*})\,\mathrm{div}\,U+Q\nabla Q\cdot U\big)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq(\Lambda-1)\bar{E}(\tau)+C\int\big(|\nabla Q|^{2}+|\nabla U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq(\Lambda-1)\bar{E}(\tau)+C\int\big(|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{Q})|^{2}+|\nabla(\widehat{X}\overline{U})|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y+C\|(\nabla\widetilde{Q},\nabla\widetilde{U})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\Lambda-1)\bar{E}(\tau)+C(\tau)+C(C_{0})\|(\nabla\widetilde{Q},\nabla\widetilde{U})\|_{L^{\infty}(B(0,C_{0}))}^{2}+C\|(\nabla\widetilde{Q},\nabla\widetilde{U})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\Lambda-1)\bar{E}(\tau)+C(\tau)+C\|(\nabla\widetilde{Q},\nabla\widetilde{U})\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}^{2}.

In the Gagliardo-Nirenberg inequality (
A.14
), choosing

\bar{r}=2,\,\,l=4,\,\,p=2,\,\,q=2,\,\,\theta=\frac{1}{4},

we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|^{\frac{1}{4}}_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\|\widetilde{Q}-Q^{*}\|^{\frac{3}{4}}_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\widetilde{Q}-Q^{*}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\widetilde{Q}-Q^{*}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|Q-Q^{*}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\widehat{X}\overline{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}+\|\nabla^{4}\widetilde{Q}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|Q-Q^{*}\|_{L^{2}}+C(\tau).

Similarly, we can also obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla\widetilde{U}\|_{L^{2}(B^{c}(0,C_{0}))}\leq C\|U\|_{L^{2}}+C(\tau).

Thus, we conclude

J_{3}\leq C\bar{E}(\tau)+C(\tau).

(6.77)

For
J_{4}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{4}

\displaystyle=C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int Q^{-\frac{1}{\delta}}\mathrm{div}\,\big(Q^{\frac{\delta}{\alpha}}\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\cdot U\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=-C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\Big(\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big(a_{1}|\nabla U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\mathrm{div}U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y\,+\int\nabla(Q^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}})\big(Q^{\frac{\delta}{\alpha}}\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\cdot U\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)

\displaystyle=-C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\Big(\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big(a_{1}|\nabla U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\mathrm{div}U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y\,-\int\frac{1}{\alpha}\big(\frac{\nabla Q}{Q}\cdot Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\mathbb{D}(U)\big)\cdot U\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big(a_{1}|\nabla U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\mathrm{div}U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y

(6.78)

\displaystyle\quad+Ce^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int\frac{|\nabla Q|^{2}}{Q^{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}|U|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big(a_{1}|\nabla U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\mathrm{div}U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\quad+Ce^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int\langle y\rangle^{-2+\delta_{\rm{dis}}-(\Lambda-2)}|U|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big(a_{1}|\nabla U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\mathrm{div}U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y+C\bar{E}(\tau),

where the last inequality follows from Lemmas
5.2
–
5.3
, the definition of
\delta_{\rm dis}
, and
\Lambda>1
.

Combining (
6.2
)–(
6.2
) yields

\frac{\rm d}{\rm d\tau}\bar{E}(\tau)+\frac{1}{2}C_{\rm{dis}}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tau}\int Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{\alpha}}\big(a_{1}|\nabla U|^{2}+(a_{1}+a_{2})|\mathrm{div}U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y\leq C\bar{E}(\tau)+C(\tau),

(6.79)

which, along with the Grönwall inequality, implies

\bar{E}(\tau)=\frac{1}{2}\int\big(|Q-Q^{*}|^{2}+|U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y\leq C(\tau).

(6.80)

Moreover, using (
2.7
), (
5.15
), and (
6.68
), we obtain

\int\big(|\nabla^{K}Q|^{2}+|\nabla^{K}U|^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}y\leq E_{K}(\tau)\leq E,

(6.81)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\|\nabla^{K+1}U(\tilde{\tau},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\,\text{d}\tilde{\tau}

(6.82)

\displaystyle\leq C(\tau)\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}e^{-\delta_{\rm{dis}}\tilde{\tau}-K\min\{\tilde{\eta},\eta\}(\tau-\tilde{\tau})}\|\phi^{\frac{K}{2}}Q^{\frac{\delta-1}{2\alpha}}\nabla^{K+1}U(\tilde{\tau},\cdot)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\,\text{d}\tilde{\tau}

\displaystyle\leq C(\tau,E).

Together with the
L^{2}
–control of
(Q-Q^{*},U)
and
\tau\geq\tau_{0}\gg E
, the uniform bound on

E_{K}(\tau)
allows us to apply the standard interpolation inequalities to obtain

\|Q-Q^{*}\|^{2}_{H^{K}}+\|U\|^{2}_{H^{K}}+\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\|U(\tilde{\tau},\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{K+1}}\,\text{d}\tilde{\tau}\leq C(\tau).

(6.83)

By the equation (
2.12
), we also derive
(
6.73
).

This completes the proof of Lemma
6.9
.
∎

7.
Global Existence of Smooth Solutions of the Reformulated Problem

In this section, we give the proof of Theorem
2.1
based on the local well-posedness obtained in Theorem
3.1
and the
a priori
estimates obtained in Proposition
